I inspected recorded execution results without rerunning the implementations.\\n\\n### arena-walkthrough\\n\\nSelecting A is supported by the actual code. Its entry retains the mounted value during disposal, then invalidates after reporting settles. Repeated teardown returns the same completion promise, and synchronous or asynchronous report failures reach the caller through rejection. B preserves the original value through a captured reference but invalidates before deferred reporting, which misses the literal temporal requirement. The judge explicitly recognized that distinction instead of treating both successful probes as equivalent evidence.\\n\\nThe synthesis coherently keeps A’s ownership and completion policy. A\\n\\nThe caller acquires one lease per mount and releases it only after the vendored deferred disposal callback reports. The release runs in `finally`, including when reporting throws. Multiple mounts of one key share the current entry. Each lease releases at most once, and deletion checks entry identity.\\n\\nCaller usage is the design boundary:\\n\\n```js\\nconst lease = acquire(key, { ready: true });\\n// Inside the disposer's callback:\\ntry { report(lease.value.ready); }\\nfinally { lease.release(); }\\n```\\n\\nThe cache exports `acquire` and `get`. `acquire` returns the value and a release capability tied to that entry. `get` preserves ordinary cache reads but does not acquire a lease. There is no public `put`, `invalidate`, replacement, or reference-count mutation. The caller cannot publish a stale deferred write. A fresh generation can start once the previous generation has no owners, and a repeated old release cannot remove it. For one key, the first value wins until all leases release. Later acquire calls do not overwrite it.\\n\\n`mount` remains the caller's only export. Its returned unmount function is idempotent, so repeated cleanup cannot queue duplicate reports. Reading the lease inside disposal also avoids accidentally reporting a different generation's value.\\n\\nRejected alternatives:\\n\\n- Restoring `setTimeout` relies on scheduler ordering and leaves cache lifetime detached from the callback that needs the value.\\n- Deleting immediately and guarding `get(key)?.ready` avoids a throw but loses the required ready report.\\n- A captured value with immediate cache invalidation reports correctly but violates retention through reporting.\\n- A compare-and-delete token without lease counting lets one unmount evict an entry another active mount still needs.\\n- Public replace and invalidate operations require extra lifecycle rules. This fixture has no replacement caller, so the API prevents replacement while an entry has owners.\\n\\nThe supplied after-state probe fails with a TypeError under Bun. The six candidate tests use the actual copied pinned disposer and pass. They cover retention during reporting, overlapping mounts, stale release after a new generation, repeated unmount, a mount created during reporting, and report failure with cleanup. `verification.json` records exact commands, outputs, and exit codes.\\n\\nThe supported callback contract is synchronous reporting, matching the fixture. An asynchronous report that returns a promise would need a separate decision about whether its settlement owns the lease. There is no claim about any public dependency or other disposer implementation. No service, installation, commit, or configuration change was performed.\\n\\nAssignment `cache-a-1` uses package freeze `f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c`. `input-digest.json` records every copied input hash and their aggregate content digest. Requested model and effort are inherited; actual values are unobserved. No parent rubric was supplied.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-07/candidate-cross-review/candidate-a/artifacts.json\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": The disposer reads that handle before releasing it.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { mount } from './implementation/caller.mjs';\\nimport { cache } from './implementation/cache.mjs';\\n\\nconst unmount = mount('panel', ready => console.log(ready));\\nunmount();\\nconsole.log(cache.get('panel').ready); // true until the disposal microtask runs\\nawait new Promise(resolve => queueMicrotask(resolve));\\nconsole.log(cache.get('panel')); // undefined\\n```\\n\\nThe complete caller is in `implementation/caller.mjs`. Its unmount callback schedules the exact supplied vendored disposer once. Inside the disposal callback, `report(entry.read().ready)` runs before `entry.release()`. A `finally` releases the entry even if reporting throws. It does not catch or suppress the reporting error.\\n\\n## Cache API\\n\\n`implementation/cache.mjs` exports `createCache()` for an independent cache and the shared `cache` used by `mount`.\\n\\n| Operation | Contract |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| `cache.acquire(key, initialValue)` | Installs a new current entry and returns its owning handle. Superseded handles retain their own value until released. |\\n| `cache.get(key)` | Returns the current value, or `undefined` when no current entry exists. |\\n| `handle.read()` | Returns this handle's value, even after another mount replaces the key. Throws after release. |\\n| `handle.replace(nextValue)` | Updates only while this handle is current and unreleased. Returns `false` for stale or released handles. |\\n| `handle.release()` | Drops this handle's value. Deletes the keyed entry only if this handle still owns it. Returns `false` after the first release. |\\n\\nFor asynchronous producers, acquire before starting work and publish through that same handle:\\n\\n```js\\nconst entry = cache.acquire('panel', { ready: true });\\nconst result = await loadPanel();\\nif (!entry.replace({ ready: true, result })) {\\n  // Another mount replaced this generation, or this mount already released it.\\n}\\n```\\n\\nThis is a replacement of the original `put/get/invalidate` contract. Migrate mount ownership and producer writes together. A stale producer must retain its handle rather than call `acquire` again when work completes.\\n\\n## Why this shape\\n\\nThe original caller disposes asynchronously and invalidates synchronously. Its callback subsequently reads `undefined.ready`. Delaying invalidation with a timer repairs that one ordering but leaves a key-reuse race. An older timer can delete a newer mount's entry.\\n\\nA handle separates a mount's retained value from whichever mount currently owns the key. Entry identity checks protect both writes and deletion. Deleting a current entry never reveals an older generation. If a newer mount unmounts first, an older live mount can still report its own ready value, but key lookup remains empty until a later mount acquires the key.\\n\\nThe public key lookup deliberately returns only the current generation. Disposal must read its own handle. A key lookup inside old teardown cannot identify which mount it belongs to.\\n\\nI considered these alternatives:\\n\\n- Moving `invalidate(key)` into the disposal callback fixes the missing value but still allows old teardown to delete a replacement.\\n- Comparing values during invalidation protects deletion but does not stop stale reads or late producer writes. Dedicated handles make all three operations use the same ownership check.\\n- Keeping a set of all generations in the cache would expose more bookkeeping than callers need. Each mount's handle already retains its generation, and release clears its value.\\n- A reference count would keep a shared entry alive but would conflate replacement with shared ownership. This fixture creates a fresh ready entry on every mount.\\n\\nValues remain ordinary JavaScript references. Callers should use `replace` rather than mutate retained objects. Reporting is synchronous, as in the supplied caller. The cache releases after `report` returns or throws; it does not await a returned promise. If a disposer never invokes its callback, its handle remains retained. The local pinned disposer always queues that callback in a microtask.\\n\\n## Verification\\n\\nRun `bun test candidate-cross-review/candidate-b/implementation/cache.test.mjs` from the fixture root.\\n\\nSeven tests cover retention during reporting, final release, old and new mount teardown in both orders, rejected stale producer writes, duplicate unmount/release, reentrant mounting during reporting, and release after a reporter throws. The last case runs in a subprocess so an uncaught reporting error cannot interfere with the test runner.\\n\\nThe original copied probe fails with `TypeError` under Bun. The candidate uses the unchanged vendored dependency bytes. These checks establish behavior for the exact local fixture only. They make no claim about a public dependency.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-07/candidate-cross-review/candidate-b/implementation/cache.mjs\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": whose handles own one generation until release. */\\nexport function createCache() {\\n  const current = new Map();\\n\\n  return {\\n    get(key) { return current.get(key)?.value; },\\n \"e781951753c4275bc4a078b7f836e1939d7c40399e8085fd146971fe4c25b443\",\n      \"bytes\": 851,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-07.json#4\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"export function createCache() {\\n  const current = new Map();\\n\\n  return {\\n    get(key) { return current.get(key)?.value; },\\n    acquire(key, initialValue) {\\n      const entry = { value: initialValue };\\n      let released = false;\\n      current.set(key, entry);\\n\\n      return {\\n        read() {\\n          if (released) throw new Error('Cache handle already released');\\n          return entry.value;\\n        },\\n        replace(nextValue) {\\n          if (released || current.get(key) !== entry) return false;\\n          entry.value = nextValue;\\n          return true;\\n        },\\n        release() {\\n          if (released) return false;\\n          released = true;\\n          if (current.get(key) === entry) current.delete(key);\\n          entry.value = undefined;\\n          return true;\\n        },\\n      };\\n    },\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nexport const cache = createCache();\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/batch-07/candidate-cross-review/selected/cache.test.mjs\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/batch-07/candidate-cross-review/selected/cache.test.mjs\",\n      \"sha256\": \"b9702c26bec15d779ea3f2462a6ffe2678daf82750b91c38db17254810e6e2c0\",\n      \"bytes\": 3768,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-07.json#4\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"import { describe, expect, test } from 'bun:test';\\nimport { createCache, cache } from './cache.mjs';\\nimport { mount } from './caller.mjs';\\n\\nconst flushDisposal = () => new Promise(resolve => queueMicrotask(resolve));\\n\\ndescribe('ownership and deferred disposal', () => {\\n  test('retains ready through reporting and releases after reporting', async () => {\\n    const observed = [];\\n    const unmount = mount('single', ready => {\\n      observed.push([ready, cache.get('single')?.ready]);\\n    });\\n    unmount();\\n    expect(cache.get('single')).toEqual({ ready: true });\\n    expect(observed).toEqual([]);\\n    await flushDisposal();\\n    expect(observed).toEqual([[true, true]]);\\n    expect(cache.get('single')).toBeUndefined();\\n  });\\n\\n  test('old callback reads its own ready value and cannot delete a replacement', async () => {\\n    const observed = [];\\n    const stopOld = mount('reused', ready => observed.push(['old', ready]));\\n    stopOld();\\n    const stopNew = mount('reused', ready => observed.push(['new', ready]));\\n    await flushDisposal();\\n    expect(observed).toEqual([['old', true]]);\\n    expect(cache.get('reused')).toEqual({ ready: true });\\n    stopNew();\\n    await flushDisposal();\\n    expect(observed).toEqual([['old', true], ['new', true]]);\\n    expect(cache.get('reused')).toBeUndefined();\\n  });\\n\\n  test('replacement unmounting first still leaves old handle readable', async () => {\\n    const observed = [];\\n    const stopOld = mount('reverse', ready => observed.push(['old', ready]));\\n    const stopNew = mount('reverse', ready => observed.push(['new', ready]));\\n    stopNew();\\n    stopOld();\\n    await flushDisposal();\\n    expect(observed).toEqual([['new', true], ['old', true]]);\\n    expect(cache.get('reverse')).toBeUndefined();\\n  });\\n\\n  test('a stale asynchronous producer cannot overwrite a replacement', () => {\\n    const local = createCache();\\n    const old = local.acquire('key', { ready: true, id: 'old' });\\n    const replacement = local.acquire('key', { ready: true, id: 'new' });\\n    expect(old.replace({ ready: false, id: 'late-old' })).toBe(false);\\n    expect(old.read()).toEqual({ ready: true, id: 'old' });\\n    expect(local.get('key')).toEqual({ ready: true, id: 'new' });\\n    old.release();\\n    expect(local.get('key')).toEqual({ ready: true, id: 'new' });\\n    expect(replacement.replace({ ready: false, id: 'updated-new' })).toBe(true);\\n    expect(local.get('key')).toEqual({ ready: false, id: 'updated-new' });\\n    replacement.release();\\n    expect(local.get('key')).toBeUndefined();\\n  });\\n\\n  test('unmount and release are idempotent', async () => {\\n    let count = 0;\\n    const stop = mount('duplicate', () => count++);\\n    stop(); stop();\\n    await flushDisposal();\\n    stop();\\n    await flushDisposal();\\n    expect(count).toBe(1);\\n    const local = createCache();\\n    const handle = local.acquire('key', 1);\\n    expect(handle.release()).toBe(true);\\n    expect(handle.release()).toBe(false);\\n    expect(handle.replace(2)).toBe(false);\\n    expect(() => handle.read()).toThrow('already released');\\n  });\\n\\n  test('report may synchronously mount a replacement without old release deleting it', async () => {\\n    let stopNew;\\n false;\\n  let completion;\\n\\n  function get() {\\n    if (disposed) throw new Error(`Entry ${String(key)} is disposed`);\\n    return value;\\n  }\\n\\n  function dispose(onDispose) {\\n    if (completion) return completion;\\n    let resolve;\\n    let reject;\\n    completion = new Promise((accept, fail) => {\\n      resolve = accept;\\n      reject = fail;\\n    });\\n    const subscription = createDisposer(async () => {\\n      try {\\n        const result = await onDispose();\\n        resolve(result);\\n      } catch (error) {\\n        reject(error);\\n      } finally {\\n        disposed = true;\\n        value = undefined;\\n      }\\n    });\\n    subscription.dispose();\\n    return completion;\\n  }\\n\\n  return Object.freeze({ get, dispose });\\n}\\n\"\n Input revision `fb7d9f00a700b1f6c3c88204d967433d99c11a73`. Method `arena`. Requested model and effort `inherit-parent`; actual model and effort `unknown` because this worker has no observed model identifier. Package freeze supplied by assignment `f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c`, installed version `0.1.0`.\\n\\nEach mount owns an entry. The entry owns its value, deferred disposal and completion promise. It exposes a read operation while disposal runs and clears its value in `finally` after the report settles. No module owns mutable cache state, and a key identifies no shared mutable slot. The caller closes over the entry rather than copying the mounted value into a disposal argument. This implements the assigned lifecycle-owned shape.\\n\\nThe exact input's disposer calls `queueMicrotask(onDispose)` and returns no completion promise. The original unmount schedules that callback and synchronously deletes the global map entry. The later callback reads `undefined.ready`. The replacement wraps the actual supplied disposer with a completion promise owned by the entry. Its callback catches synchronous throws and asynchronous rejection from report, rejects completion with the original error and clears the entry in `finally`. Consumers should await or catch unmount's returned promise. Ignoring a rejection can still produce the runtime's unhandled rejection behavior.\\n\\nDouble unmount returns the same promise and never schedules a second disposal. Disposing two entries with the same key cannot overwrite or delete the other's value. The probe deliberately gives them different values and lets one finish while the other waits. It also checks overlapping calls through the actual mount API. Entries remain readable until their own report completes; reads after completion throw an explicit disposed-entry error. I rejected passing a captured value into disposal because that is seat B's assigned alternative. mount(key, report) {\\n  const entry = createEntry(key, { ready: true });\\n  return () => entry.dispose(() => report(entry.get().ready));\\n}\\n\\nFILE cache.mjs\\nimport { createDisposer } from '../../../committed-inputs/additional/cache/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\\n\\nexport function createEntry(key, initialValue) {\\n  let value = initialValue;\\n  let disposed = false;\\n  let completion;\\n\\n  function get() {\\n    if (disposed) throw new Error(`Entry ${String(key)} is disposed`);\\n    return value;\\n  }\\n\\n  function dispose(onDispose) {\\n    if (completion) return completion;\\n    let resolve;\\n    let reject;\\n    completion = new Promise((accept, fail) => {\\n      resolve = accept;\\n      reject = fail;\\n    });\\n    const subscription = Actual model and effort are unknown because the assignment exposes no observed runtime model receipt.\\n\\nEach mount captures its own freshly allocated value and passes it to `disposeMounted`. Disposal never looks up a key. The cache belongs to the caller that invokes `createCache`; the module contains no mutable cache singleton. Usage is `const cache = createCache(); const unmount = mount(cache, key, report);`.\\n\\nThe pinned disposer queues its callback with `queueMicrotask` and returns no completion handle. Unmount queues disposal, then invalidates synchronously. The value remains available through the callback's closure even after the cache entry disappears. The explicit `disposeMounted(value, report)` boundary documents which mount's value disposal receives. This candidate does not move invalidation into a disposal completion callback.\\n\\n`invalidate` deletes only when the current entry is the same object the unmount owns. Every mount creates a distinct object, so an older same-key mount cannot delete a newer entry. The latest mount replaces the cache lookup value. Unmounting that latest mount clears the lookup; it does not restore an older active mount. Both mounts still dispose their own captured values. Two cache owners can use the same key independently.\\n\\nUnmount is idempotent before and after the deferred callback runs. The guard is set before scheduling, so repeated calls queue only one callback. A throwing `report` propagates through the real vendored microtask as an uncaught exception. Cache invalidation has already completed and repeated unmount still does nothing. This candidate deliberately does not swallow the report exception. The exact pinned disposer has no synchronous scheduling failure or async report contract; neither is claimed as supported. If report starts asynchronous work, that work's rejection belongs to report's caller contract.\\n\\nAlternatives rejected:\\n\\n- A module-level Map leaks ownership across independent callers and permits key collisions.\\n- Restoring a timer in invalidation makes correctness depend on scheduling order and still lets old unmounts delete new same-key entries.\\n- Reading from an owner-local Map during disposal still selects whichever mount most recently wrote the key.\\n- A lifecycle entry that invalidates after disposal completion is seat A's assigned shape. Seat B keeps disposal's required value independent of cache lifetime.\\n- A general lease registry or reference count adds shared lifecycle bookkeeping this fixture does not need. This review assesses selection and implementation quality; it assigns no case grade or host status. I inspected recorded execution results without rerunning the implementations.\\n\\n### arena-walkthrough\\n\\nSelecting A is supported by the actual code. Its entry retains the mounted value during disposal, then invalidates after reporting settles. Repeated teardown returns the same completion promise, and synchronous or asynchronous report failures reach the caller through rejection. B preserves the original value through a captured reference but invalidates before deferred reporting, which misses the literal temporal requirement. The judge explicitly recognized that distinction instead of treating both successful probes as equivalent evidence.\\n\\nThe synthesis coherently keeps A’s ownership and completion policy. The graft is a hand-adapted remount-before-disposal test from B; it does not introduce B’s immediate invalidation or uncaught microtask error policy. Rejecting a global map and timer-based deletion follows the demonstrated ownership and scheduling failure.\\n\\nVerification is substantive: the probe checks pinned vendor bytes against the committed input and digest, deferred execution, repeated teardown, independent same-key lifetimes, invalidation and failure handling. Both synthesis and graft verification records show exit `0`. These establish the exercised local behavior, not compatibility with an arbitrary disposer. A vendor that never calls back, or reporting that never settles, can retain the entry indefinitely. Callers must handle teardown rejection.\\n\\nThe native order supports a fresh later judge: B and A terminate at orders `25` and `26`, parent inspection follows at `29–30`, and the distinct judge starts at `31`. Judgment completes at `37`, before synthesis at `43`.\\n\\nThe exploration’s breadth is constrained: the coordinator assigned both whole shapes in advance. This is a comparison of those alternatives, not evidence that the workers independently discovered the design space.\\n\\n### candidate-cross-review\\n\\nThe selected A implementation directly satisfies the required lifecycle ordering. Its entry owns value retention, the actual deferred disposer and completion. The distinct-value overlap probe is particularly useful: it demonstrates separate ownership beyond two callbacks that both report `true`.\\n\\nB is meaningfully different from A and from walkthrough B: it retains an explicit caller-owned map with identity-guarded invalidation. That guard prevents an older mount from deleting a newer value, but does not restore an older active value after the newest mount unmounts. It also reports after invalidation and exposes more coordination surface. Those are concrete reasons to prefer A. The judge’s rejection of B’s immediate invalidation, map plumbing and uncaught report-error policy is consistent with the implementation.\\n\\nThe synthesis preserves A and grafts coverage of both same-key teardown orders plus remount-before-disposal. It avoids combining incompatible ownership policies. Recorded synthesis and graft probes exit `0`, including reproduction of the committed original failure, vendor pinning, asynchronous lifetime retention and cleanup after report errors.\\n\\nThe selected implementation assumes the pinned disposer’s scheduling behavior. Its `subscription.dispose()` call sits outside a scheduling-failure cleanup path; a synchronous scheduling exception would leave its completion unresolved. The supplied vendor normally queues a microtask, so the existing report-failure checks do not establish this additional behavior.\\n\\nThe separate trials are honestly bounded:\\n\\n- Native evidence records the unavailable-worker spawn rejection, `agent thread limit reached`. No worker artifact is substituted for that rejection.\\n- The dropout worker actually executes the authorized failure command and exits `7`. Its `failure.json` records no candidate and excludes integration. The worker completes its report while the task assignment remains blocked.\\n- The survivor contains a real lifecycle proposal, implementation, rationale and probe. Its parent-check record exits `0`. It remains one survivor, with no competitive selection, judge or trial synthesis claimed.\\n\\nMain candidates terminate at orders `50` and `54`; parent inspection occurs at `55–56`; the fresh judge starts at `57`, completes at `60`, and synthesis follows at `62`. The failure exercises remain separate from that main comparison.\\n\\n### boundary-design\\n\\nA is the better fit for the stipulated package. Its boundaries call the pure domain function directly, the facade contains export declarations, and no construction mechanism is needed for this stateless calculation. B’s factory, injected calculator, capability object and HTTP dependency on `batch.ts` add maintenance knowledge without a demonstrated production variation or lifecycle requirement. The judge carefully distinguishes unnecessary surface and ambiguous dependency compliance from an invented literal-test failure.\\n\\nBoth candidates provide caller-first examples, module ownership, complete rationale sections and all eight red-flag assessments. The comparison acknowledges duplicated acceptance policy rather than claiming that private validators automatically create one invariant owner.\\n\\nThe implementation follows the selected architecture:\\n\\n- `domain/order.ts` contains the required structural type and pure multiplication.\\n- HTTP owns parsing, validation and response formatting.\\n- Batch validates and calls `total` directly.\\n- The facade exports `handle`, `runBatch` and domain `Order`.\\n- The consumer uses domain orders; `legacy.ts` is absent.\\n\\nThe useful grafts are B’s concrete invoice example and scoped dependency verification. The synthesis rejects B’s factory rather than adding an import-policy framework to police an abstraction it did not retain.\\n\\nVerification goes beyond transpilation. Recorded baseline, isolated-domain and final compiler executions use installed TypeScript with strict, no-emit configuration and exit `0`. Final Bun execution records four tests, zero failures and 49 assertions. The shared boundary corpus exercises both actual boundaries, while native batch checks cover non-JSON numeric values and frozen-input immutability. Source inspection supports the required dependency directions.\\n\\nTwo evidence-quality gaps remain:\\n\\n- The synthesized documents retain copied candidate-stage language, including unchecked workflow steps and statements that verification is proposed or unobserved, despite the completed implementation and recorded checks. The implementation evidence is stronger than the final narrative’s consistency.\\n- `no-legacy-references.json` scans only domain and batch. It supports their dependency restrictions, but its filename overstates the search scope. My inspection of the remaining implementation files found no legacy dependency.\\n\\nNative ordering supports terminal inspection before fresh judgment: candidates finish at `69` and `74`, inspection occurs at `76`, the judge starts at `77` and finishes at `81`, and synthesis/implementation follows at `82`. No additional checkpoint was requested. No observed design failure required a scrap cycle.\\n\\n### Shared evidence limits\\n\\n`evidence/native/index.json` identifies distinct native worker threads, all observed as `gpt-6.1-sol` with medium effort. Requests inherit the parent; this supports worker independence, not model diversity.\\n\\nReceipt `method` and `resultMethod` objects are coordinator normalization of one observed artifact-producing attempt. Original worker returns say `arena`; they are not native structured method objects. Pending owner reservations were resolved after spawn and should remain distinguishable from final observed identities.\\n\\nThe assignment-event label `completed-and-inspected` conflates terminal completion with later inspection. Separate native observations show inspection before every judge, so the label is imprecise without reversing the relevant ordering.\\n\\nThe requested installed `0.1.0` orchestration-contract path is currently absent. I read its archived copy at `evidence/source/references/orchestration-contract.md`. That supports review against the preserved contract, not current installed discovery.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-02/candidate-cross-review/parent-check-a.json\",\n createDisposer } from '../../committed-inputs/additional/cache/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\\n\\nexport function createEntry(key, initialValue) {\\n  let value = initialValue;\\n  let disposed = false;\\n  let completion;\\n\\n  function get() {\\n    if (disposed) throw new Error(`Entry ${String(key)} is disposed`);\\n    return value;\\n  }\\n\\n  function dispose(onDispose) {\\n    if (completion) cross-survivor, method arena.\\n\\nInput revision fb7d9f00a700b1f6c3c88204d967433d99c11a73. Package freeze f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c. Actual model and effort are unknown.\\n\\nEach mount owns a lifecycle entry. The entry retains its value during deferred disposal, invalidates it after the reporting callback settles, and returns one completion promise for all teardown calls. There is no module-level map or mutable global state.\\n\\nThe executable proposal is cache.mjs and caller.mjs. Run `bun candidate-cross-review/trials/survivor/probe.mjs` from the task repository. No judge verdict or graft is claimed.\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-02/candidate-cross-review/trials/survivor/rationale.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-02/candidate-cross-review/trials/survivor/rationale.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"54c647474a33534396c3b5e64bd5671695f40c8c573f22cead8cbe0223550d81\",\n      \"bytes\": 3082,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-02.json#2\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"Assignment cross-survivor, input fb7d9f00a700b1f6c3c88204d967433d99c11a73, method arena.\\n\\nThe original caller writes a global key, schedules a callback through the actual vendored `queueMicrotask` disposer, then deletes the key synchronously. The callback reads undefined and throws. Restoring a timer delays deletion but leaves ownership and overlapping same-key mounts unresolved.\\n\\nThe assigned lifecycle-owned shape puts value retention, disposal completion and invalidation in one entry. The caller supplies the reporting callback. Each disposal callback reads its entry's current value when the vendor invokes it. The entry clears that value only after the callback settles. The cache owns this policy, so callers cannot accidentally choose an earlier invalidation point.\\n\\nRepeated unmount returns the same promise and schedules only one vendor callback, including calls made after completion or rejection. Same-key mounts do not share entries, so one mount cannot replace or invalidate another's value. This changes global key lookup semantics. If cross-mount sharing becomes a real requirement, this design needs an explicit owner and borrowing contract.\\n\\nThe entry catches synchronous report exceptions and rejected report promises, rejects completion with the original error and clears the value in finally. Teardown callers must handle rejection. A report promise that never settles retains its value indefinitely. Reentrant teardown returns the existing promise, but a report that awaits its own teardown promise would deadlock and is outside the callback contract. The supplied vendor cannot throw synchronously from dispose under normal operation. Defensive handling still rejects completion and clears the value if it does. No claim covers a different public dependency or vendor version.\\n\\nI rejected a global map with delayed deletion because it depends on scheduling and shares key ownership. Parent derives 3-6 gradeable criteria.\",\n      \"Judge scores by criterion and recommends a base; parent reads every candidate end to end and resolves disagreement before selection.\",\n      \"Parent records base, hand-adapted grafts, rejections, convergence or reframing, dropouts and verification.\",\n      \"Observe independent worker operations, result artifacts and permission-bound writes; record requested and actual models separately.\",\n      \"One survivor means incomplete competition, never a full arena pass.\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"Two or more isolated candidates finish before a fresh readonly judge receives the rubric and labelled artifact paths. Parent reads and scores all candidates, compares rationales, selects a maintainable base, grafts coherently and verifies the synthesis with an adjacent evidence note.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"Without independent workers or a fresh judge, authorized sequential alternatives and parent comparison may produce a useful synthesis labelled reduced capability. Dropouts are recorded; one survivor is incomplete competition and does not prove competitive selection.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"Required independence, exact model choice, source reads, isolation or writes unavailable or denied blocks that gate. Zero surviving artifacts blocks synthesis. Missing required principle or contract blocks dependent execution.\",\n    \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n      \"Sequential role switching proves independent candidates or independent review.\",\n      \"Requested model names prove actual model diversity.\",\n      \"A judge begins while candidates still write.\",\n      \"One survivor establishes full competition.\",\n      \"Structural validation or a case definition establishes host pass.\",\n      \"The rubric is supplied to candidates.\",\n      \"Upstream autonomy permits external writes or implicit setup.\"\n    ]\n  },\n  \"sourceFiles\": [\n    \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/arena/SKILL.md\"\n  ]\n}\n"

      "path": "fixtures/cases/candidate-cross-review/outputs/arena-source-contracts.raw.json",
      "bytes": 25852,
      "sha256": "da01e055eb2f40804af244ee2e6d1dffaa72f1e32cf7d8529c66445382d8ab2b",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"tool\": \"exec_command\",\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat skills/arena/SKILL.md references/host-capabilities.md references/model-roles.md references/orchestration-contract.md references/workflow-ownership.md\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 12000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"4fb986\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000005875,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 6275,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: arena\\ndescription: \\\"Spawn N parallel candidates at the same task, pick a base, graft the strongest parts of the losers into it. Use for /arena, 'arena this', 'throw it in the arena', or when one attempt at a non-trivial artifact would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Arena\\n\\nRead [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Use only exposed, authorized operations. Read each applied principle leaf in full before dependent work. Missing required reads block that work. Invocation aliases are guidance, not proof of registered host commands.\\n\\nIndependent candidates and a fresh independent judge require observed worker operations and inspected artifacts. If unavailable, produce authorized sequential alternatives and parent comparison only as reduced capability. Do not claim independence, background execution, model diversity or a full arena pass. If independence is required, leave that gate blocked.\\n\\nFan out N parallel attempts at the same task. Read every candidate end to end. Pick the strongest as the base. Graft the best ideas from the others into it. Verify the synthesized result.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen an exposed todo list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Without one, use an authorized local Markdown checklist and disclose the reduced binding.\\n\\n1. Frame\\n2. Fan out\\n3. Cross-judge\\n4. Pick\\n5. Graft\\n6. Verify\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\nThe N candidates will receive the same prompt, so the prompt is the contract.\\n\\n1. State the artifact each candidate is producing.\\n2. Derive the rubric. State what success looks like for *this* task, then turn it into 3-6 concrete gradeable criteria. The rubric is the picker's tool in Phase D. Candidates only see the task.\\n3. Pick the runners. Use an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena runners` preference if available. Otherwise request two inherited workers, one per seat. The source defaults used two specific model families. Their source-host slugs are not portable availability evidence. Select an explicit model or effort only from current exposed documentation or observed enumeration under the model-role contract. An `auto` or `inherit-parent` entry means omit explicit model selection. On rejection, record it and disclose a supported same-family alternative when available, otherwise an authorized inherited alternative. If the exact model is required, block instead. Record requested and observed model and effort separately, using unknown when unobserved. More seats can cover multiple design directions. Same model N times is appropriate when work is generation-bound rather than judgment-sensitive. Unknown models leave diversity unverified.\\n4. Assign output paths. Each candidate writes to its own authorized location, a git worktree where supported, otherwise a distinct local candidate directory. Follow [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Confirm isolation and write permissions before dispatch. Record immutable assignment identity, input SHA, owner, role and evidence links under the assignment contract. Workers never integrate shared files. Worktree creation and persistence require observed authorized capabilities.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nDispatch all N independent candidates together through the exposed worker operation, using observed background execution when available. Give each the same task, shared grounding path, its isolated output path, and instructions to produce both the artifact and a short rationale. Withhold the rubric from candidates. Inspect terminal results and artifact paths before accepting completion; a wait notification alone is insufficient.\\n\\nEach rationale names the alternatives the candidate considered and what it rejected.\\n\\nIf a candidate fails to produce output, proceed with N-1 and note the dropout in the synthesis record. One survivor means incomplete competition, even if its artifact is useful. Zero survivors blocks synthesis. Never integrate partial or cancelled artifacts as completed candidates.\\n\\n## Phase C: Cross-judge\\n\\nAfter all Phase B candidates reach terminal state and their accepted artifacts are inspected, choose one model from an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena cross-judge pool` preference. Without that preference, use an inherited worker or a supported explicit selection under the model-role contract. The source pool named the same two families as the runners; it does not establish portable model availability. Prefer a different observed model family from the parent's when available. Disclose rejected choices and supported fallbacks, or block if the exact choice is required.\\n\\nSpawn one fresh readonly judge worker. It sees the rubric and completed candidates by path label, scores each criterion, and recommends a base with rationale. It runs in parallel with the parent's reading in Phase D when observed background execution permits it, never with candidates still writing. The judge has no candidate write ownership. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified. Unavailable fresh judging remains a reduced or blocked gate, never an invented independent verdict.\\n\\n## Phase D: Pick a base\\n\\nRead every candidate end to end before picking.\\n\\nScore each candidate against the rubric criterion by criterion, not on holistic feel. Compare against the cross-judge. Agreement on the base confirms the pick. Disagreement means one of you is biased or the rubric was ambiguous. Read both rationales before deciding.\\n\\nPick the base on which candidate a future maintainer can extend most easily without breaking invariants. Prefer the cleaner boundary or smaller API when two feel tied, per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md).\\n\\nRecord the pick and the reason in a short synthesis note alongside the base artifact, including the cross-judge's verdict.\\n\\n## Phase E: Graft\\n\\nWalk each losing candidate once more and identify what is worth porting into the base. The signal is usually one or two things per candidate, not most of it.\\n\\nFold each graft in by hand, per [redesign-from-first-principles](../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md). Don't paste mechanically. The result has to remain coherent under one mental model.\\n\\nRecord what was grafted, from which candidate, and what was rejected and why.\\n\\nWhen N candidates converge on the same shape, that is a strong agreement signal. Note the convergence in the record and deliver the consensus shape within authorized scope. No graft is needed. When N candidates wildly diverge, Phase A was under-specified. Reframe and re-run rather than averaging the divergence.\\n\\n## Phase F: Verify\\n\\nThe synthesized artifact has to hold up under the same scrutiny as any other output, per [prove-it-works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\\n\\nIf verification surfaces a problem the arena did not catch, either Phase A was wrong (re-frame and re-run) or one candidate caught it and you missed the graft (go back to Phase E). Don't paper over.\\n\\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to the synthesis prose. External publication, commits, messages and setup require their own authorization.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nOne synthesized artifact. One short synthesis note alongside, naming the base, the grafts and their source candidates, the rejections, the dropouts if any, and the verification result.\\n# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "text": "Implement an owned local cache lifecycle module and caller compatible with the supplied cache/caller/probe context. Preserve synchronous invalidation and deferred disposal.       "text": "{\n  \"tool\": \"exec_command\",\n  \"arguments\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat outputs/candidate-A/{cache.mjs,caller.mjs,lifecycle.test.mjs,rationale.md,result.json,verification.json} outputs/candidate-B/{cache.mjs,caller.mjs,cache.test.mjs,rationale.md,verification.md}\",\n    \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/candidate-cross-review\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 10000\n  },\n  \"return\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"0b843f\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000023125,\n    \"exit_code\": 1,\n    \"original_token_count\": 2500,\n    \"output\": \"const entries = new Map();\\nexport function put(key, value) { entries.set(key, value); }\\nexport function get(key) { return entries.get(key); }\\nexport function invalidate(key, expectedValue) {\\n  if (arguments.length === 1 || entries.get(key) === expectedValue) {\\n    entries.delete(key);\\n  }\\n}\\nimport { createDisposer } from './vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\\nimport { put, invalidate } from './cache.mjs';\\nexport function mount(key, report) {\\n  const value = { ready: true };\\n  put(key, value);\\n  const subscription = createDisposer(() => report(value.ready));\\n  let disposed = false;\\n  return () => {\\n    if (disposed) return;\\n    disposed = true;\\n    subscription.dispose();\\n    invalidate(key, value);\\n  };\\n}\\ncat: outputs/candidate-A/lifecycle.test.mjs: No such file or directory\\nEach mount retains its value in the disposal callback. Deferred disposal therefore reads the readiness of that mount even when its key has been deleted or reused. The unmount closure sets its disposed flag before scheduling disposal, so repeated calls schedule at most one report. Cache deletion remains synchronous. Conditional invalidation compares value identity to preserve replacement entries; a one-argument invalidate retains the original unconditional API.\\n\\nRejected alternatives:\\n- Restoring timer-based invalidation changes the required synchronous behavior and permits stale deletion of a replacement.\\n- Looking up the key inside the callback can throw after deletion or report another mount.\\n- Capturing only a readiness boolean would lose changes to the owned value before its callback runs.\\n- Modifying the disposer violates the exact pinned dependency contract.\\n- Adding generation tokens adds another identity mechanism when mount already creates a unique value object. The cache expects ownership by object identity for conditional invalidation.\\n\\nScope is the supplied local fixture only. No public dependency behavior is claimed. The vendored file is copied unchanged. No shared inputs or other candidates were edited. Requested model and effort are inherited; actual model and effort are unknown.\\n{\\n  \\\"assignmentId\\\": \\\"arena-A\\\",\\n  \\\"inputRevision\\\": \\\"83555355d932b1c3cbb33e4a1b300568a39264e0\\\",\\n  \\\"method\\\": \\\"arena\\\",\\n  \\\"status\\\": \\\"completed\\\",\\n  \\\"implementationVerified\\\": true,\\n  \\\"evidenceLimit\\\": \\\"Initial four read-only raw tool envelopes not captured locally. return entries.get(key); }\\n// With an expected value, only the current owner may invalidate the key.\\nexport function invalidate(key, expected) {\\n  if (arguments.length === 1 || entries.get(key) {\\n    if (disposed) return;\\n    disposed = true;\\n    subscription.dispose();\\n    invalidate(key, value);\\n  };\\n}\\nimport { test, expect } from 'bun:test';\\nimport { mount } from () => {});\\n  const replacement = get('replacement');\\n  await flush();\\n  expect(reports).toEqual([false]);\\n  expect(get('replacement')).toBe(replacement);\\n  unmountNew();\\n  await flush();\\n});\\n\\ntest('stale unmount preserves replacement and repeated calls dispose once', async () => {\\n  const reports = [];\\n  const oldUnmount = mount('stale', value => reports.push(value));\\n  get('stale').ready = false;\\n  const newUnmount = mount('stale', value => reports.push(value));\\n  const replacement = get('stale');\\n  oldUnmount();\\n  oldUnmount();\\n  expect(get('stale')).toBe(replacement);\\n  await flush();\\n  expect(reports).toEqual([false]);\\n  newUnmount();\\n  newUnmount();\\n  expect(get('stale')).toBeUndefined();\\n  await flush();\\n  expect(reports).toEqual([false, true]);\\n});\\n\\ntest('ordinary cache API remains compatible', () => {\\n  const value = { ready: true };\\n  put('api', value);\\n  expect(get('api')).toBe(value);\\n  expect(invalidate('api', {})).toBe(false);\\n  expect(get('api')).toBe(value);\\n  expect(invalidate('api')).toBe(true);\\n  expect(get('api')).toBeUndefined();\\n});\\n\\ntest('vendored disposer is byte identical to pinned dependency', async () => {\\n  const bytes = await Bun.file(new URL('./vendor/disposer-v1.mjs', import.meta.url)).arrayBuffer();\\n  expect(new Bun.CryptoHasher('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex')).toBe('d33e8d36600ad5df008657e478e39f9100a347098b4176f37923e5056cb3427f');\\n});\\nAssignment: arena-B\\nInput revision: 83555355d932b1c3cbb33e4a1b300568a39264e0\\nMethod: arena candidate; no integration or nested workers.\\nRequested model and effort: inherit-parent. Observed model and effort: unknown.\\n\\nThe supplied diff makes invalidation synchronous. The real caller schedules its disposer callback as a microtask and then deletes the entry before that callback reads get(key).ready. The original after-state therefore throws. Restoring the timer delays deletion until after the microtask, but also delays invalidation and can delete a replacement entry.\\n\\nEach mount now holds its own mutable value reference for deferred reporting. Optional expected-value invalidation deletes only if that value still occupies the key. Existing unconditional invalidate(key) calls retain their original behavior. A per-mount guard ensures at most one call to the exact unchanged vendored disposer. Distinct mount objects act as ownership tokens without a separate registry.\\n\\nRejected alternatives: optional chaining would hide the missing entry and report undefined; reading the latest cache entry would report another mount's value; restoring setTimeout would violate synchronous invalidation and retain stale-deletion risk; replacing or mocking the disposer would evade the pinned dependency; a separate generation-token map adds redundant lifecycle state for this supplied object-valued caller.\\n\\nScope is the exact local fixture, with no claim about a public package. Tests exercise the actual vendored dependency, synchronous removal, deferred own-value reporting, replacement preservation, and repeated unmounts.\\nAssignment arena-B, method arena, revision 83555355d932b1c3cbb33e4a1b300568a39264e0.\\n\\nObserved verification:\\n- `bun test outputs/candidate-B/cache.test.mjs`: exit 0, 5 pass, 0 fail, 15 assertions.\\n- `bun outputs/candidate-B/grounding/probe.mjs`: exit 1, TypeError reading get(key).ready. Follow exact keys/types. method must be structured {sampleCount:positive integer,sampleDefinition:nonempty string,order:nonempty string}, not text. For example declare candidate method describing exact observable cache tests, same method passed in assignment and independently echoed in worker RESULT.JSON. Judge has its own method describing exact criterion scoring and must echo it too. Assignment resultRevision must be actual e07d98d277a3c055b09f5b5736626ec9db21cbad; resultMethod must be EXACT object actually returned by that worker. Do not repair missing old metadata by inventing it. If missing actual metadata, retain invalid result and permit at most ONE actual retry to same worker. Never overwrite original attempts. OutputPath is a concrete RESULT.JSON file, not directory. Parent SHA256 hashes the FINAL non-self-referential RESULT.JSON after worker writes it. Put program source text and test execution receipts in RESULT.JSON if helpful to portable evidence, also execute app/tests from actual files. Hashes alone do not prove behavior.\n\nTo bind ownership BEFORE work: preallocate distinct absolute worker output directories under ./outputs/{candidate-a,candidate-b,judge}; create actual fresh worker in BOOTSTRAP-ONLY mode first, asking it not to work until task dispatch. Use observed returned canonical worker identity. Append immutable assignment (id,scope,inputRevision,outputOwner=actualworkerId,isolatedOutputPath,role,retryCount=0,terminalStatus=pending,evidenceLinks) to append-only assignments.jsonl BEFORE followup-task dispatch. Give both candidate workers SAME task artifact path ./outputs/candidate-task.md and same task/grounding, and their separate assignments/path/method metadata. Include full role prompt, exact source location and requirement to create own checklist BEFORE any task read/work, then FULL mode and required leaf reads. Neither candidate may read parent rubric or peer output. Dispatch BOTH candidate work followups before either reaches terminal; never sequential role play. Workers write only exact assigned directories. Actual worker method metadata belongs in its returned RESULT.JSON; returned identity/revision/method/artifact proofs must be real.\n\nInspect every terminal candidate result, source/tests/rationale end to end and verify owned outputs plus matching metadata. Real Bun tests/probes required. THEN fresh read-only judge bootstrap, immutable assignment and dispatch after both candidates terminal and inspected. Judge may return JSON in final response and coordinator capture exact bytes in judge/RESULT.JSON (explicit read-only judge capture allowance). It must receive rubric, completed labelled paths and exact revision/method, full role/checklist/mode requirements. Judge scores criterion by criterion with rationale. Record actual model if observed else unknown, requested inherit-parent. No model-diversity claim without observations. Parent reads and scores all, resolves disagreements, selects base, hand-grafts coherent improvements or records genuine convergence, names rejections, verifies synthesis and writes adjacent note.\n\nThis case ALSO requires actual candidate dropout and unavailable-worker branch. Exercise a separate third actual candidate worker that reports unable to complete or is cancelled through observed operation, retain terminal evidence and exclude it from two surviving candidates. Exercise unavailable-worker handling using actual observed rejection/unavailability if exposed, otherwise retain that variant as unverified/blocked; do NOT fabricate operation denial or deliberately request unauthorized unavailable model. One survivor never full competition. Document separately any controlled scenario branch versus actual host availability.\n\nExact standalone receipt shape: {schemaVersion:1,kind:'arena',inputRevision:'e07d98d277a3c055b09f5b5736626ec9db21cbad',assignments:[...],reviewArtifact:string,reviewedBy:string,declaredOrder:integer,candidates:[id,id],judge:judgeAssignmentIdString,rubricArtifact:string,synthesis:{order:integer,inspectedCandidates:[id,id],artifact:string,verificationArtifact:string}}. Assignment EXACT keys are in strict-schema-reference.txt; no extras. Include judge in assignments. Integer order is observed monotonically increasing append-only events, declared before actual dispatch. candidate completedOrder < judge startedOrder, synthesis order > judge completedOrder. Distinct output parent directories, distinct actual worker IDs; taskArtifact same for candidates, rubricProvided false candidates true judge. Judge is read-only on candidate artifacts. All resultRevision/resultMethod values taken from actual worker outputs, never retrospective normalization. Keep record of bound successfulOperations spawn/inspect-result/isolated-write (judge no isolated-write required), assignmentId/evidence exact RESULT.JSON path, and inspect-method for receipt; these annotate actual operations, never substitute for them. Full receipt does not prove qualitative success.\n\nWrite ./outputs/method-receipt.json, ./outputs/successful-operations.json, ./outputs/events.jsonl with actual append-only chronology, ./outputs/artifact-manifest.json with inspected paths/hashes, ./outputs/completion.json with supplied freeze, task SHA, observed models, tool and permission receipts, missing predicates. Preserve raw relevant tool captures or exact native transcript locator (own transcript only), and all failed attempts. Do not search other private sessions. Final provide paths and factual outcomes, no self-awarded pass.\n"
    },

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import {writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nconst id=process.argv[2];\nlet scripts;\nif(id==='candidate-a')scripts=['baselines/after/probe.mjs','baselines/before/probe.mjs','tests/normal.mjs','tests/immediate.mjs','tests/replacement.mjs','tests/repeated-errors.mjs','proposed/probe.mjs'];\nelse throw Error('Explicit inspected script list required');\nconst receipts=scripts.map((path,index)=>{const command=['bun','--no-install',`outputs/${id}/${path}`];const p=Bun.spawnSync(command,{stdout:'pipe',stderr:'pipe'});return {order:index+1,command,exitCode:p.exitCode,stdout:p.stdout.toString(),stderr:p.stderr.toString()}});\nwriteFileSync(`outputs/${id}-parent-execution.json`,JSON.stringify(receipts,null,2));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(receipts,null,2));\nif(receipts.some((r,i)=>r.exitCode!==(i===0?1:0)))process.exit(1);\n"

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import assert from 'node:assert/strict';\nimport {readFileSync,writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {createHash} from 'node:crypto';\nconst vendorHash=createHash('sha256').update(readFileSync('outputs/synthesis/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs')).digest('hex');\nassert.equal(vendorHash,JSON.parse(readFileSync('input/manifest.json')).dependency.sha256);\nconst specs=[\n {args:['input/probe.mjs'],expected:1,output:'teardown error: TypeError'},\n {args:['outputs/before-state/probe.mjs'],expected:0,output:'disposed ready: true\\nteardown completed'},\n ...['normal','immediate','replacement','repeat-and-errors'].map(s=>({args:['outputs/synthesis/scenarios.mjs',s],expected:0,output:'PASS'})),\n {args:['outputs/synthesis/additional-checks.mjs'],expected:0,output:'Completion waits for a controlled asynchronous report to fulfill.'},\n {args:['outputs/synthesis/probe.mjs'],expected:0,output:'disposed ready: true\\nteardown completed'},\n];\nconst receipts=specs.map((spec,index)=>{const command=['bun','--no-install',...spec.args],p=Bun.spawnSync(command);return {order:index+1,command,exitCode:p.exitCode,expectedExitCode:spec.expected,stdout:p.stdout.toString(),stderr:p.stderr.toString(),expectedOutputFragment:spec.output}});\nconst result={scope:'Exact local fixture only',vendorHash,receipts,errors:receipts.flatMap(r=>[...(r.exitCode===r.expectedExitCode?[]:['Unexpected exit '+r.order]),...(r.stdout.includes(r.expectedOutputFragment)?[]:['Missing expected output '+r.order])]),knownLimit:'Own-completion dependency cycle observed pending at two microtask checkpoints. No cycle or timeout policy is implemented.'};\nwriteFileSync('outputs/synthesis-verification.json',JSON.stringify(result,null,2)+'\\n');\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(result,null,2));\nassert.deepEqual(result.errors,[]);\n"

    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/run-scenario.mjs",
      "bytes": 1007,
      "sha256": "6b0273301a4a99b0e792ac1af2ef139f5bbde237919c884d75103db9e6109be3",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { mkdir, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport { join } from 'node:path';\nconst [id, script, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);\nif (!id || !script) throw new Error('Expected receipt ID and script');\nconst root = import.meta.dir;\nawait mkdir(join(root, 'receipts'), { recursive: true });\nconst path = join(root, 'receipts', id);\nawait mkdir(path, { recursive: false });\nconst command = [process.execPath, '--no-install', script, ...args];\nconst run = Bun.spawnSync(command, { cwd: root, stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });\nconst stdout = new TextDecoder().decode(run.stdout);\nconst stderr = new TextDecoder().decode(run.stderr);\nawait writeFile(join(path, 'stdout.txt'), stdout);\nawait writeFile(join(path, 'stderr.txt'), stderr);\nawait writeFile(join(path, 'receipt.json'), JSON.stringify({ command, cwd: root, exitStatus: run.exitCode, stdout: 'stdout.txt', stderr: 'stderr.txt' }, null, 2) + '\\n');\nprocess.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({ id, exitStatus: run.exitCode, stdout, stderr }) + '\\n');\n"
    },
