    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/RATIONALE.md",
      "bytes": 7718,
      "sha256": "30e908ce2deee3a9b4c52acc70b3a8fc45c181660bb734f5bfc873b13c3d00c2",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Generation-owned teardown\n\n## Problem\n\nThe fixture's deferred disposal callback reads a key after synchronous invalidation deleted it. The supplied after-state probe exits 1 with a TypeError. The restored timer probe exits 0 and reports true. Both observations appear in receipts/01-after and receipts/02-before. Restoring a timer would delay invalidation and retain key-based ownership mistakes.\n\n## Usage, caller's view\n\nUse `const unmount = mount(key, report)` and `await unmount()`. The cache entry disappears during the unmount call. The report runs later. The promise resolves after report finishes, including a returned promise, or rejects with report's original error. Repeated calls return that same promise and do not report again. DESIGN.md records three caller examples written before the implementation and the corresponding signatures.\n\n## Shape\n\nThe cache privately maps each key to a generation object holding a value. `put` returns a lease with that value and `release()`. Every put creates a distinct generation, even when two puts receive the same value object. Release deletes only when its generation is still current. `get` reads the current value. `invalidate` keeps its unconditional synchronous key-wide contract. Both deletion operations return a boolean.\n\nThe mount callback reads `lease.value.ready`, so a missing key or newer generation cannot change which mount it reports. Unmount queues the exact pinned disposer callback and releases the lease before returning. A local started flag permits one queue operation. The callback converts synchronous throws and returned promise rejections into rejection of the one completion promise. Cache deletion already happened, so callback failure cannot retain or delete a replacement. Report side effects are not retried or rolled back.\n\nThe lease hides generation comparison behind one method. The caller retains the subscription's scheduling and report policy. That boundary keeps the cache independent of the vendored disposer. No exported internal map or generation token exists. JavaScript does not provide compile-time state guarantees here. A fresh private envelope and closure enforce generation identity at runtime.\n\n## Synthesis decision\n\nThis candidate selects the generation lease after comparing it with cache-owned retirement in DESIGN.md. Parent-owned arena selection, independent judging, grafting, and integration have not happened in this worker. The two local sketches are sequential alternatives and establish no independent perspective or model diversity.\n\n## Tradeoffs accepted\n\n- We accept a returned lease in exchange for cleanup that cannot delete another generation.\n- We accept a promise-returning teardown in exchange for an observable report failure contract. The supplied caller ignored teardown's return value, so its normal use still runs. Callers that need failure handling must await or catch the returned promise.\n- We accept a retained value reference in exchange for reading the mount's value after cache eviction. The lease retains the same object, not a deep snapshot. This fixture creates a separate `{ ready: true }` object for each mount.\n- We accept caller coordination of subscription and lease in exchange for a cache that has no subscription policy.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\nCache-owned retirement would store disposer and completion state with each generation and expose `register(...).retire()`. It hides more lifecycle coordination, but it couples a general cache to report callbacks and this disposer. The local fixture does not establish that every cache write needs that lifecycle. It lost on extension cost for plain put/get consumers.\n\nRestoring the timer is a point fix rather than a whole ownership design. It changes immediate invalidation behavior and still lets old callbacks and timers address the newer generation. A null guard would report undefined or skip the report and leave value ownership unresolved. Neither meets this task's observed reporting and ownership requirements.\n\n## Verification\n\nThe required six scenarios ran from actual .mjs files with `bun --no-install` in the declared order. Each receipt preserves the actual command, stdout, stderr, and exit status. The first probe failed as expected. The remaining five commands exited 0. The four proposed scenarios assert deferred true reporting, synchronous deletion, same-key replacement before and after queueing, same-object reuse, idempotent teardown, reentrant teardown, synchronous report exceptions, and rejected report promises. The pinned vendor hash matched the manifest before tests. There is no public-package or broader runtime claim.\n\nThe recorder initially failed to create its missing parent directory before either baseline probe launched. recorder-failure-attempt-0.json preserves that setup failure. After fixing only the recorder directory setup, the six scenario receipts were captured. Existing receipt directories are exclusive and cannot be overwritten by rerunning the recorder.\n\n## Principles that changed the design\n\nModel the Domain and Foundational Thinking selected a generation record before logic. Separate Before Serializing Shared State gave each mount its own lease while preserving one current-key lookup. Exhaust the Design Space required the retirement alternative before implementation. Make Operations Idempotent selected one teardown promise and one disposal queue operation. Boundary Discipline placed callback error conversion at the report boundary. Encode Lessons in Structure put replacement checks inside release rather than in every caller. Laziness Protocol and Minimize Reader Load kept two small implementation modules and the existing cache function names. Fix Root Causes rejected timer restoration and null guards. Build the Lever and Prove It Works produced run-scenario.mjs and retained real receipts. Test Behavior, Not Implementation selected observable reports and cache results. Sequence Work into Verifiable Units put baseline observations before implementation. Every named principle's frozen leaf was read in full.\n\nA supplemental artifact check initially passed an ArrayBuffer to the crypto hash API and failed. artifact-check-failure-attempt-0.json preserves the failure. verify-artifacts.mjs uses Buffer input and performs the corrected check. This failure did not affect the six scenario receipts.\n\n## Open questions and risks\n\nShould a future consumer receive a deeply immutable value, or retain mutable object identity? The fixture establishes only a boolean report from a separately created value. That question does not block this local design. Report callbacks that never settle leave teardown pending. Code that ignores a rejected teardown promise can trigger an unhandled rejection. These are explicit completion semantics rather than hidden swallowing of callback failures.\n\n## Next implementation step\n\nThe executable candidate is complete locally. The parent should inspect source, receipts, and competing candidate artifacts before selecting or integrating a design.\n\n## Attention\n\nreviewed by unknown\n\nNo independent reviewer ran inside this worker. Nested workers are forbidden. Parent review remains pending. create-skill, deslop, and matching control-cli companions are not exposed. The no-comments reviewer, interrogate review, architect multi-perspective gate, and cross-model trail review are unsatisfied here. Bun executes the actual local fixture, but does not claim a companion control check. Prose received direct unslop and technical-writing checks. No exported full transcript was available, so the trail check is limited to this turn's visible work. No commits, configuration, installation, PR, or external action occurred.\n"
    },

      "text": "# Candidate B checklist\n\nLocal Markdown is the reduced todo binding. The initial checklist was created before task reads. The detailed workflow checklist was expanded after reading the mode.\n\n## Bug fix steps, verbatim\n\n1. Reproduce it yourself on the matching surface via the control skill in the mode's Non-negotiables, even when a debug or instrumentation protocol says to ask the user to reproduce. Ask the user only with a stated, specific reason the control surface cannot reach the target, and only after driving it as far as it goes. If it won't reproduce directly, synthesize the trigger, tighten conditions, or instrument until it fires. Record the exact symptom, trigger, surface, revision, command or action, exit status where available, and output before changing production code. Missing control companions or denied execution leave the original reproduction unverified. A proxy check cannot satisfy the matching-surface gate.\n2. Binary-search the cause. Form candidate hypotheses, then rule them out until one survives. Seed them with [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem and [why](../../why/SKILL.md) for regression history, using their complete prompts and source contracts. Each pass takes the split that cuts the most remaining problem space, gets runtime evidence and eliminates candidates. When program state is unclear, add authorized instrumentation or logging and read it as the code runs. Don't guess. Drive a long or stubborn hunt with an exposed, authorized repeated-execution operation. If unavailable, perform bounded in-session iterations and disclose reduced capability, without claiming a background loop. Confirm the surviving mechanism with runtime evidence before the step-3 architect/interrogate fan-out.\n3. Plan the fix. If it crosses a function boundary, [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) first, using its complete exploration, runner, rationale and screening contracts. Contested design follows the mode's [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) gate. Delegate implementation to a subagent using the configured bug-fix role with a specific scope. The portable default is inherited model and effort. The canonical default `grok-4.7-xhigh-fast` is historical provenance, not current availability. Select a configured model only when supported by exposed tools and observed; retain rejected selections and disclosed fallbacks. Unknown actual models prove no diversity. No required independent operation means that gate remains blocked.\n4. Verify on the same surface. The original repro now passes. Retain the same trigger and conditions and record the actual output after the bounded root-cause fix. \"Inconclusive\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Unit tests show branch behavior, not bug absence. Keep denied controls, missing companions and unrun checks explicit.\n5. Stage the commits so the failing repro lands before the fix in git history. See [tdd](../../tdd/SKILL.md) for the failing-test-first cadence when the bug has a cheap local test path. Skip adding that test when it would be expensive, integration-heavy or unclear, and state the reason and closest executable regression check. This is the canonical [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, the failing test first and the fix on top. Commits, rebases and history changes require actual authorization and available operations. Without them, preserve the failing-before/passing-after artifacts and ordered delivery plan; do not claim that sequence landed.\n6. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) in full when its dependency and required capabilities are available and authorized. Missing playbook or companion gates stop dependent delivery. Return checked local artifacts and the exact unperformed actions otherwise. PR creation and other external writes require explicit authorization. Opening a PR does not implicitly start Babysit.\n\n## Step outcomes\n\n- [x] Reproduce on the actual fixture with Bun. Matching control-cli companion remains unavailable.\n- [x] Trace queueMicrotask ordering with the exact vendor and compare restored timer. Historical motivation remains unknown.\n- [x] Ground, sketch two whole designs, compare, and implement one candidate. Nested delegation is forbidden, so these sketches have no independent-review claim.\n- [x] Verify the same mount/unmount trigger and additional ownership and error scenarios.\n- [x] Preserve failing-before and passing-after artifacts. Skip commits because assignment forbids commits.\n- [x] Read Opening a PR. Skip external delivery, branches, configs, and installs because assignment forbids them.\n\n## Architect phases\n\n- [x] Ground\n- [x] Sketch\n- [x] Agree. Implementation is already authorized. Parent owns synthesis.\n- [x] Implement\n- [x] Scrap. Skip because no architectural contradiction arose during implementation.\n\n## Throughput checkpoint\n\n- Blocking first steps. Own checklist preceded task reads. Full task, fixture, mode, architecture contracts, and applied leaves were read before implementation. After-state and before-state probes preceded candidate implementation.\n- Independent workstreams. n/a inside this worker. Parent owns parallel candidates and judgment.\n- Shared mutable state. Only candidate-b files are written. Runtime map belongs to one cache module. Entry generations separate mount ownership under a shared lookup key.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. One worker owns coupled cache and caller code. No nested workers are authorized.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n- [x] Read every input file end to end and verify vendor hash.\n- [x] Write caller usage and signatures before implementation.\n- [x] Compare generation lease and cache-owned retirement designs.\n- [x] Run six scenarios in assigned order with separate stdout, stderr, and exit receipts.\n- [x] Preserve recorder setup failure separately. No scenario launched during that failed attempt.\n- [x] Write rationale and RESULT.JSON.\n- [x] Read back outputs and perform self-check. Independent review remains parent-owned.\n"

      "text": "const entries = new Map();\nexport function put(key, value) { entries.set(key, value); }\nexport function get(key) { return entries.get(key); }\nexport function invalidate(key) { setTimeout(() => entries.delete(key), 0); }\n"

      "text": "// Exact vendored fixture dependency implementation, pinned by local SHA-256 in manifest.json.\nexport function createDisposer(onDispose) {\n  return { dispose: () => queueMicrotask(onDispose) };\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/TRACE.md",
      "bytes": 3983,
      "sha256": "04bc51e3d41a4b91987f1dbdd824f2fce5bebe4ca3ff890cd0bbf758479709fc",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Local fixture trace\n\n## Overview\n\nThe supplied after-state throws during teardown on this Bun run. The timer before-state reports true. Receipts 01-after and 02-before record the actual probes from owned copies. Both use the exact vendored disposer bytes pinned in input/manifest.json.\n\n## Key concepts\n\nThe map stores the latest value for a key. The supplied caller's disposer callback reads the map at callback time. The pinned disposer only queues that callback using queueMicrotask. It does not guard repeat disposal or catch callback exceptions.\n\n## How it works\n\nIn input/caller.mjs, mount stores `{ ready: true }` and returns unmount. Unmount calls dispose first. That queues the callback but does not execute it. Unmount then calls invalidate. In the supplied after-state, invalidate deletes the key before the call stack ends. The queued callback calls get(key), receives undefined, and `.ready` throws. This is deterministic ordering within the local probe, rather than two threads racing.\n\nIn the restored before-state, invalidate schedules a zero-delay timer. The queued microtask reads the value before that timer removes the entry. This observed success relies on delayed deletion and lookup by key. If a newer mount replaces the key before the old callback runs, that callback could read the newer value. The timer also deletes whichever generation is current when it executes. Those replacement outcomes are source-derived risks, not baseline probe observations.\n\n```text\nsupplied after\nunmount -> queue callback -> delete key -> return -> callback -> get missing key -> throw\nrestored before\nunmount -> queue callback -> queue timer -> return -> callback -> read true -> timer delete\nproposed\nunmount -> queue callback -> release own generation -> return -> callback -> read own value\n```\n\n## Where things live\n\ninput/cache.mjs owns the lookup map. input/caller.mjs owns mount and teardown. input/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs defines the exact local scheduling behavior. input/invalidation.diff changes only timer deletion to direct deletion. after and before contain executable owned copies. proposed contains the candidate implementation.\n\n## Gotchas\n\nRestoring the timer changes immediate lookup behavior. A null guard would hide the missing owner without guaranteeing a true report. Looking up by key inside the deferred callback also confuses generations. The repair gives the deferred callback a reference to its own value and gives teardown a generation-specific release operation.\n\n## Why constraints and evidence gaps\n\nThe question is why timer removal breaks teardown and whether the timer's historical purpose is known. The code anchor is input/cache.mjs lines 1 through 4, input/caller.mjs lines 1 through 8, and input/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs lines 1 through 4. The exact input revision is e07d98d277a3c055b09f5b5736626ec9db21cbad, verified with git rev-parse HEAD.\n\nThe mechanical cause is directly supported by those files and the two probe receipts. Historical author intent is unknown. A timer may have masked this lifetime dependency. That is inference from the ordering, not a documented motivation. A pure cache implementation defect was a competing hypothesis. The actual caller and pinned queued callback rule it out as the whole explanation.\n\nSource control history was not searched beyond current HEAD identity. The assignment forbids other versions. No PR record is supplied. Issue tracker, long-form external documents, team chat, infrastructure observability, error tracking, and analytics are outside the authorized local fixture and were not searched. The supplied input README and diff were read fully. This is a scoped direct how explanation and reduced why investigation, without independent investigators.\n\nPreserve synchronous invalidation. Change the callback's value owner and generation-specific cleanup. Avoid a timer-based contract or null guard. The remaining risk is that callers must observe the returned promise if report fails.\n"

After-state and before-state probes preceded candidate implementation.\\n- Independent workstreams. n/a inside this worker. Parent owns parallel candidates and judgment.\\n- Shared mutable state. No nested workers are authorized.\\n\\n## Deliverables\\n\\n- [x] Read every input file end to end and verify vendor hash.\\n- [x] Write caller usage and signatures before implementation.\\n- [x] Compare generation lease and cache-owned retirement designs.\\n- [x] Run six scenarios in assigned order with separate stdout, stderr, and exit receipts.\\n- [x] Preserve recorder setup failure separately. No scenario launched during that failed attempt.\\n- [x] Write rationale and RESULT.JSON.\\n- [x] Read back outputs and perform self-check. Independent review remains parent-owned.\\n`;\nawait writeFile(join(root, 'CHECKLIST.md'), checklist);\nconst method = {\n  sampleCount: 6,\n  sampleDefinition: 'Six observable scenarios run with Bun from actual files: supplied after-state probe; restored timer before-state probe; proposed normal teardown reports true; immediate invalidation removes current entry; same-key replacement survives old teardown; repeated teardown and callback failure policy are asserted.',\n  order: 'Run baseline after-state then before-state, then normal teardown, immediate invalidation, same-key replacement, and repeated teardown with callback failure assertions. Preserve stdout stderr exit status for every command.',\n};\nconst ids = ['01-after', '02-before', '03-normal', '04-immediate', '05-replacement', '06-repeat-errors'];\nconst receipts = [];\nfor (const id of ids) {\n  const path = join(root, 'receipts', id);\n  const receipt = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(path, 'receipt.json'), 'utf8'));\n  const stdout = await readFile(join(path, 'stdout.txt'), 'utf8');\n  const stderr = await readFile(join(path, 'stderr.txt'), 'utf8');\n  receipts.push({ id, ...receipt, stdoutText: stdout, stderrText: stderr, artifact: relative(root, join(path, 'receipt.json')) });\n}\nif (receipts[0].exitStatus !== 1 || receipts.slice(1).some(r => r.exitStatus !== 0)) throw new Error('Unexpected scenario status');\nconst artifacts = [];\nasync function list(dir) {\n  for (const entry of await readdir(dir)) {\n    const path = join(dir, entry);\n    if ((await stat(path)).isDirectory()) await list(path);\n    else if (!['RESULT.JSON'].includes(entry)) artifacts.push(relative(root, path));\n  }\n}\nawait list(root);\nconst result = {\n  assignmentId: 'candidate-b', workerId: '/root/candidate_b',\n  inputRevision: 'e07d98d277a3c055b09f5b5736626ec9db21cbad',\n  method, requestedModel: 'inherit-parent', actualModel: 'unknown',\n  requestedEffort: 'inherit-parent', actualEffort: 'unknown',\n  outputOwner: '/root/candidate_b', isolatedOutputPath: root,\n  role: 'candidate', retryCount: 0, terminalStatus: 'completed',\n  artifacts,\n  rationale: 'RATIONALE.md',\n  design: 'DESIGN.md', tracedModel: 'TRACE.md',\n  executionReceipts: receipts,\n  priorAttempt: 'recorder-failure-attempt-0.json',\n  failedAttempts: ['recorder-failure-attempt-0.json', 'artifact-check-failure-attempt-0.json'],\n  supplementalVerification: 'receipts/07-artifact-check/receipt.json',\n  exactVendorSha256: 'd33e8d36600ad5df008657e478e39f9100a347098b4176f37923e5056cb3427f',\n  limitations: [\n    'Scope is the exact local fixture on observed Bun 1.4.2. No public dependency claim.',\n    'Actual model and effort are unobserved. No model diversity claim.',\n    'This worker compared sequential sketches. Parent owns arena selection, judge, grafting, integration and independent review.',\n    'Nested workers are forbidden. Other versions, private memory, peer outputs and external systems were not consulted.',\n    'Teardown returns a promise. Callers must observe its rejection. No complete exported transcript audit was performed.',\n  ],\n};\nawait writeFile(join(root, 'RESULT.JSON'), JSON.stringify(result, null, 2) + '\\n');\nconst readback = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(root, 'RESULT.JSON'), 'utf8'));\nfor (const path of readback.artifacts) await stat(join(root, path));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify({ resultReadBack: true, artifactsExist: readback.artifacts.length, orderedScenarios: receipts.map(r => ({ id: r.id, exitStatus: r.exitStatus })) }));\n"

    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/DESIGN.md",
      "bytes": 4125,
      "sha256": "29d54f96e3a1477eb99d58e0663988232d4876be2d67d5af4447a51d8571d114",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Cache ownership design\n\n## Usage, written before the signatures\n\nA component keeps one mount handle. Teardown removes its own entry before returning. The returned promise exposes callback failure.\n\n```js\nimport { mount } from './proposed/caller.mjs';\nconst unmount = mount('panel', ready => console.log(ready));\nawait unmount();\n```\n\nOverlapping mounts can use the same lookup key. An old handle reports its own value and cannot remove the newer mount.\n\n```js\nconst oldUnmount = mount('panel', reportOld);\nconst newUnmount = mount('panel', reportNew);\nawait oldUnmount();\nawait newUnmount();\n```\n\nA direct cache consumer owns a generation through its lease. Explicit invalidation remains a synchronous, key-wide operation.\n\n```js\nimport { put, get, invalidate } from './proposed/cache.mjs';\nconst lease = put('panel', { ready: true });\nget('panel');\ninvalidate('panel');\nlease.release();\n```\n\n## Shape\n\nThe data shape is a private `Map<key, generation>`. Each generation contains one value. A put creates a fresh generation even when the same value object is reused. A lease exposes that value and an identity-checked `release`. The caller owns a single completion promise and a single started flag.\n\n```js\n// cache.mjs\nexport function put(key, value) { throw new Error('not implemented'); }\n// Returns { value, release(): boolean }.\nexport function get(key) { throw new Error('not implemented'); }\n// Returns the current value or undefined.\nexport function invalidate(key) { throw new Error('not implemented'); }\n// Removes the current key synchronously and returns boolean.\n\n// caller.mjs\nexport function mount(key, report) { throw new Error('not implemented'); }\n// report(ready) may return a value or promise.\n// Returns unmount(): Promise<void>, the same promise on repeated calls.\n```\n\n## Whole ownership designs compared before implementation\n\n| Design | Storage and ownership | Callback reads | Deletion and errors | Interface depth |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Generation lease, selected | Cache owns current-generation map. Each caller owns its lease. | Lease value belongs to that mount. | Lease release synchronously removes only that generation. Caller turns report failure into teardown promise rejection. | Existing put/get/invalidate plus a returned lease hide generation identity. Caller retains disposer and report policy. |\n| Cache-owned retirement | Cache owns generation, disposer, callbacks, and completion state. `register(key,value,onRetire)` returns `{ retire() }`. | Callback receives its retired value as an argument. | `retire()` detaches current generation synchronously, then queues its report. Error rejects retirement promise. | A smaller caller API hides the entire lifecycle, but cache gains knowledge of the pinned disposer and callback reporting. |\n\nThe selected lease design keeps a general local cache independent of the fixture's subscription lifecycle. The retirement design would be preferable if every cache write carried the same disposal policy. That requirement is absent from the supplied fixture. These are sequential sketches within this candidate, not independent designs or an arena verdict.\n\n## Red flag screen\n\nThe lease design has one map writer in cache.mjs. Caller code cannot delete a specific generation by reconstructing its identity. It can only invoke its returned lease. `invalidate` intentionally addresses the current key, while `release` addresses one generation. They have different contracts. The caller must still coordinate disposal and lease release. That is an accepted shallow-boundary risk, bounded to one caller here. The retirement alternative hides that coordination but couples cache writes to reports and the disposer. Neither sketch adds pass-through modules or a mirrored registry. Ordinary `.mjs` exports provide no package-level import restrictions. The private map remains lexical and unexported.\n\n## Decision\n\nImplement the generation lease and promise-returning unmount. Do not restore the timer. Preserve synchronous key-wide invalidation and synchronous generation release. The parent retains synthesis and integration ownership.\n"
    },

      "text": "import { createDisposer } from './vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\nimport { get, put, invalidate } from './cache.mjs';\nexport function mount(key, report) {\n  put(key, { ready: true });\n  const subscription = createDisposer(() => report(get(key).ready));\n  return () => { subscription.dispose(); invalidate(key); };\n}\n"

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { mkdir, readFile, writeFile, copyFile } from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport { join } from 'node:path';\nimport { createHash } from 'node:crypto';\nconst out = import.meta.dir;\nconst input = join(out, '../../input');\nfor (const name of ['after', 'before']) {\n  await mkdir(join(out, name, 'vendor'), { recursive: true });\n  for (const file of ['cache.mjs', 'caller.mjs', 'probe.mjs', 'vendor/disposer-v1.mjs']) {\n    await copyFile(join(input, file), join(out, name, file));\n  }\n}\nconst after = await readFile(join(input, 'cache.mjs'), 'utf8');\nconst before = after.replace('entries.delete(key);', 'setTimeout(() => entries.delete(key), 0);');\nif (before === after) throw new Error('Removed timer line was not restored');\nawait writeFile(join(out, 'before/cache.mjs'), before);\nawait mkdir(join(out, 'proposed/vendor'), { recursive: true });\nawait copyFile(join(input, 'vendor/disposer-v1.mjs'), join(out, 'proposed/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs'));\nconst bytes = await readFile(join(input, 'vendor/disposer-v1.mjs'));\nconst actual = createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex');\nconst manifest = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(input, 'manifest.json'), 'utf8'));\nif (actual !== manifest.dependency.sha256) throw new Error('Vendor pin mismatch');\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify({ copiedFixture: true, beforeChange: 'restore removed setTimeout line only', vendorSha256: actual }));\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/proposed/scenarios.mjs",
      "bytes": 4415,
      "sha256": "edc2adf54727dbacd2a816f5114bc9be7a4bb33c4129f0ed39ab30c9ce2136af",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import assert from 'node:assert/strict';\nimport { mount } from './caller.mjs';\nimport { get, put, invalidate } from './cache.mjs';\n\nconst scenario = process.argv[2];\nif (scenario === 'normal') {\n  const reports = [];\n  const unmount = mount('normal', ready => reports.push(ready));\n  assert.equal(get('normal').ready, true);\n  const done = unmount();\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, []);\n  await done;\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [true]);\n  assert.equal(get('normal'), undefined);\n  console.log('PASS normal teardown reports [true] after deferred callback');\n} else if (scenario === 'immediate') {\n  const lease = put('direct', { ready: true });\n  assert.equal(get('direct').ready, true);\n  assert.equal(invalidate('direct'), true);\n  assert.equal(get('direct'), undefined);\n  assert.equal(lease.release(), false);\n  let reported;\n  const unmount = mount('immediate', ready => { reported = ready; });\n  assert.equal(get('immediate').ready, true);\n  const done = unmount();\n  assert.equal(get('immediate'), undefined);\n  assert.equal(reported, undefined);\n  await done;\n  assert.equal(reported, true);\n  const invalidatedUnmount = mount('admin', ready => { reported = ready; });\n  assert.equal(invalidate('admin'), true);\n  await invalidatedUnmount();\n  assert.equal(reported, true);\n  console.log('PASS immediate invalidate and unmount remove current entry before return');\n} else if (scenario === 'replacement') {\n  const reports = [];\n  const oldUnmount = mount('replacement', value => reports.push(['old', value]));\n  const newUnmount = mount('replacement', value => reports.push(['new', value]));\n  const current = get('replacement');\n  const oldDone = oldUnmount();\n  assert.equal(get('replacement'), current);\n  await oldDone;\n  assert.equal(get('replacement'), current);\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [['old', true]]);\n  await newUnmount();\n  assert.equal(get('replacement'), undefined);\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [['old', true], ['new', true]]);\n  const value = { ready: true };\n  const first = put('same-object', value);\n  const second = put('same-object', value);\n  assert.equal(first.release(), false);\n  assert.equal(get('same-object'), value);\n  assert.equal(second.release(), true);\n  const retainedReports = [];\n  const retainedUnmount = mount('different-value', ready => retainedReports.push(ready));\n  const replacement = put('different-value', { ready: false });\n  await retainedUnmount();\n  assert.deepEqual(retainedReports, [true]);\n  assert.equal(get('different-value').ready, false);\n  assert.equal(replacement.release(), true);\n  const queuedUnmount = mount('queued-replacement', ready => retainedReports.push(ready));\n  const done = queuedUnmount();\n  const queuedLease = put('queued-replacement', { ready: false });\n  await done;\n  assert.equal(get('queued-replacement').ready, false);\n  assert.deepEqual(retainedReports, [true, true]);\n  assert.equal(queuedLease.release(), true);\n  console.log('PASS same-key replacements survive old teardown, including same-value identity and queued replacement');\n} else if (scenario === 'repeat-and-errors') {\n  const reports = [];\n  const unmount = mount('repeat', ready => reports.push(ready));\n  const done = unmount();\n  assert.equal(unmount(), done);\n  await done;\n  assert.equal(unmount(), done);\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [true]);\n  for (const kind of ['throw', 'reject']) {\n    const failure = new Error(`report ${kind}`);\n    let calls = 0;\n    let failingUnmount;\n    failingUnmount = mount(kind, ready => {\n      calls += 1;\n      assert.equal(ready, true);\n      assert.equal(failingUnmount(), failingDone);\n      if (kind === 'throw') throw failure;\n      return Promise.reject(failure);\n    });\n    assert.equal(get(kind).ready, true);\n    const failingDone = failingUnmount();\n    assert.equal(get(kind), undefined);\n    assert.equal(failingUnmount(), failingDone);\n    await assert.rejects(failingDone, error => error === failure);\n    assert.equal(failingUnmount(), failingDone);\n    await assert.rejects(failingUnmount(), error => error === failure);\n    assert.equal(calls, 1);\n    const recovery = mount(kind, ready => reports.push(ready));\n    await recovery();\n  }\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [true, true, true]);\n  console.log('PASS repeated teardown reports once and returns same promise; sync throws and rejected reports reject it without retaining cache entries');\n} else {\n  throw new Error(`Unknown scenario ${scenario}`);\n}\n"

    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/proposed/caller.mjs",
      "bytes": 780,
      "sha256": "026b444e819ce47824006882a0d77b3b5c60f467d587a355920672e4ad4aff74",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { createDisposer } from './vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\nimport { put } from './cache.mjs';\n\nexport function mount(key, report) {\n  const lease = put(key, { ready: true });\n  let resolveCompletion;\n  let rejectCompletion;\n  const completion = new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveCompletion = resolve;\n    rejectCompletion = reject;\n  });\n  const subscription = createDisposer(() => {\n    try {\n      Promise.resolve(report(lease.value.ready)).then(\n        () => resolveCompletion(),\n        rejectCompletion,\n      );\n    } catch (error) {\n      rejectCompletion(error);\n    }\n  });\n  let started = false;\n  return function unmount() {\n    if (!started) {\n      started = true;\n      subscription.dispose();\n      lease.release();\n    }\n    return completion;\n  };\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/candidate-b/proposed/cache.mjs",
      "bytes": 415,
      "sha256": "caecdb40208c618d6e92bdd85e113fee84f7c27769876abf041d8aa8308200da",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "const entries = new Map();\n\nexport function put(key, value) {\n  const generation = { value };\n  entries.set(key, generation);\n  return Object.freeze({\n    value,\n    release() {\n      if (entries.get(key) !== generation) return false;\n      return entries.delete(key);\n    },\n  });\n}\n\nexport function get(key) {\n  return entries.get(key)?.value;\n}\n\nexport function invalidate(key) {\n  return entries.delete(key);\n}\n"
    },

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "// Exact vendored fixture dependency implementation, pinned by local SHA-256 in manifest.json.\nexport function createDisposer(onDispose) {\n  return { dispose: () => queueMicrotask(onDispose) };\n}\n"

      "text": "import {readFileSync,writeFileSync,appendFileSync,mkdirSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {resolve} from 'node:path';\nconst root=process.cwd(), out=resolve('outputs');\nconst source='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3';\nconst sha='e07d98d277a3c055b09f5b5736626ec9db21cbad';\nconst role=readFileSync('worker-role.md','utf8');\nconst method={sampleCount:6,sampleDefinition:'Six observable scenarios run with Bun from actual files: supplied after-state probe; restored timer before-state probe; proposed normal teardown reports true; immediate invalidation removes current entry; same-key replacement survives old teardown; repeated teardown and callback failure policy are asserted.',order:'Run baseline after-state then before-state, then normal teardown, immediate invalidation, same-key replacement, and repeated teardown with callback failure assertions. Preserve stdout stderr exit status for every command.'};\nwriteFileSync(out+'/method.json',JSON.stringify(method,null,2));\nwriteFileSync(out+'/candidate-task.md',`# Shared candidate task\\n\\nInput revision ${sha}. CWD ${root}. Frozen package ${source}. Only this package version may be read. No memory, old output, grades, other package versions or sessions.\\n\\nProduce an independent executable local cache API repair/design, rationale and tests. Read every file under ./input end to end. Review invalidation.diff against after-state cache.mjs and actual caller. Use exact pinned vendored disposer. Reproduce after-state and timer before-state from owned copies, identify microtask ordering and compare at least two whole ownership designs before implementing your chosen one. Preserve synchronous invalidation unless you justify a different explicit contract. Explain same-key replacement ownership, repeated teardown and callback errors. Scope every claim to local fixture. Do not invent broader dependency behavior.\\n\\nUse actual .mjs files and Bun with --no-install. No installs, configs, commits, external actions or nested workers. Write only your assigned absolute directory. Never read parent rubric, peer directories, parent review, judge output or private memory. Same shared task for both candidates.\\n\\nBefore task reads/work create your own checklist in assigned directory. Then read FULL frozen skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md including Principles index and every applicable leaf. Read frozen README, docs/workflows/arena.md via its actual README link, skills/arena/SKILL.md and references/{host-capabilities,model-roles,workflow-ownership,orchestration-contract}.md. Read how and architect, runner prompt/rationale template/design-red-flags and applicable principles before code. Fulfill full role prompt below. Parent owns arena orchestration; you own one candidate, must not spawn or run competing controllers. Ground directly as simple how explainer and provide traced model. Declare missing companion gates honestly. No independent-review claim for self-review.\\n\\nUse method EXACTLY as assigned, independently echo actual inputRevision and method in your FINAL RESULT.JSON. Metadata must come from actual work. RESULT.JSON keys include assignmentId, workerId (observed canonical identity), inputRevision, method, requestedModel:'inherit-parent', actualModel:'unknown' unless observed, artifacts, rationale, executionReceipts, limitations. Include source text/test receipts for portability if useful. Do not put self hash in RESULT.JSON. Never overwrite failed attempts.\\n\\nMethod\\n\\n${JSON.stringify(method,null,2)}\\n\\n# Full worker role prompt\\n\\n${role}\\n\\n# Architect runner contract\\n\\nRead ${source}/skills/architect/references/runner-prompt.md in full and apply its full design discipline. Include caller usage first, shape/signatures, alternatives and rationale using its template. Synthesis decision remains parent-owned.\\n`);\nwriteFileSync(out+'/rubric.md',`# Parent-only rubric\\n\\nScore each criterion 0 to 4, where 0 lacks evidence, 1 is materially flawed, 2 has a usable approach with important gaps, 3 satisfies the criterion with minor limitations, and 4 has direct thorough evidence.\\n\\n1. Ordering diagnosis. Explains actual caller and pinned queueMicrotask behavior; observed failing after-state and successful timer before-state, without generalizing to public dependency.\\n2. Ownership and lifecycle correctness. Normal teardown reports true; synchronous invalidation remains meaningful; same-key replacement and repeated teardown cannot corrupt current ownership; failure policy explicit.\\n3. Caller clarity and extension cost. Small coherent API, explicit resource lifetime, understandable usage and low maintenance burden; compare whole designs.\\n4. Behavioral evidence. Actual Bun source/tests exercise assertions including replacement and callback failure; receipts identify commands, exits and outputs; no mocks substituting pinned dependency.\\n5. Claim and scope discipline. Grounded rationale distinguishes verified behavior from limits, preserves vendor bytes, names rejected alternatives and avoids hidden timer contracts.\\n`);\nappendFileSync(out+'/checklist.md','\\n## Arena phases, verbatim\\n\\n- [ ] 1. Frame\\n- [ ] 2. Fan out\\n- [ ] 3. Cross-judge\\n- [ ] 4. Pick\\n- [ ] 5. Graft\\n- [ ] 6. Verify\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Full reads, exact SHA, exposed APIs, rubric and isolation precede dispatch.\\n- Independent workstreams. Two candidate directories and separate controlled dropout.\\n- Shared mutable state. Parent alone writes assignments, events and synthesis.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. Two complete ownership designs, one later read-only judge.\\n');\nwriteFileSync(out+'/events.jsonl',JSON.stringify({order:1,event:'declared',at:new Date().toISOString(),inputRevision:sha,source,details:'Parent-only rubric and shared task written before candidate dispatch.     {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/parent-grounding.md",
      "bytes": 2516,
      "sha256": "9ed75ad6ae1c384685f8e28e753a9c39b337411dc5748dc1c9e0b1fce409a6dd",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Cache lifetime problem\n\nThe map in input/cache.mjs owns entries keyed by caller-provided keys. mount writes a new object and registers a disposer callback that later reads get(key).ready. The returned unmount calls dispose then invalidate. The pinned disposer queues the callback with queueMicrotask. Synchronous invalidate therefore removes the object before the callback dereferences it. The former setTimeout deletion runs after the microtask, so it masks this lifetime coupling.\n\nA repair must decide whether a subscription owns a captured resource or a key lookup at callback time. Capturing the mount resource can preserve teardown after immediate deletion. Same-key replacement is a separate ownership problem. An older mount must not delete a newer mount's entry. Repeated teardown and callback failure need explicit behavior. Input only establishes the local fixture, not intended semantics of any public dependency.\n\nThe simple how exploration is performed directly by the parent from all seven supplied input files. No separate explainer worker was used. The two candidate packages perform the parallel design exploration under Arena. Architect is an advisory design discipline within the active Arena controller, not a second controller.\n\n# Principles applied\n\nSeparate Before Serializing Shared State gives each worker a distinct directory. Fix Root Causes requires reproducing microtask invalidation ordering. Model the Domain requires an explicit entry ownership model. Laziness Protocol favors the smallest coherent API when tied. Redesign From First Principles governs grafting. Test Behavior, Not Implementation requires observable assertions. Prove It Works requires parent execution against actual files. Build the Lever supplies a rerunnable verification script. Exhaust the Design Space requires comparing whole ownership designs.\n\n# Reduced or missing companion bindings\n\nNo task-list operation is exposed, so outputs/checklist.md is the reduced todo binding. create-skill, no-comments and a verified different-model trail reviewer are not exposed. No companion installation is authorized. No commit is authorized, so deslop and PR delivery are inapplicable. Matching CLI or UI controls are inapplicable to this module-only API. Parent and worker actual models are unknown. Cross-model review is unverified. The own-run transcript has not been exposed as a local file or native history operation; a complete transcript audit cannot be claimed. Tool captures are retained in outputs instead.\n"
    },

      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Parent candidate comparison\n\nThese scores compare candidate artifacts for base selection. They do not grade workflow execution.\n\n| Criterion | Candidate A | Candidate B |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Ordering diagnosis | 4. Actual caller and pinned microtask ordering traced; both baselines observed. | 4. Same direct evidence, with deterministic event-loop ordering stated precisely. |\n| Ownership and lifecycle | 4. Fresh record per put, private conditional deletion, retained value, repeat and reentrant completion. | 4. Equivalent generation ownership plus adopted report promises; repeat and replacement assertions hold. |\n| Caller clarity and extension cost | 3. Small implementation and lazy completion allocation, but synchronous report restriction requires a future contract extension. Handle invalidation and key invalidation use the same word for different authorities. | 4. release addresses owned generation and invalidate addresses current key. One returned completion handles synchronous and asynchronous reports without another public option. Eager promise and started flag are a modest internal cost. |\n| Behavioral evidence | 4. Six assigned scenarios and unchanged original probe, rerun by parent. Includes same-object reuse and reentrant cleanup. | 4. Six assigned scenarios, rerun by parent. Includes different ready values, queued replacement, synchronous throw and rejected report promise. Two tooling failures preserved. |\n| Claim and scope discipline | 4. Exact local vendor scope; async limitation explicit; no public-package or diversity claim. | 4. Exact local scope and completion caveats explicit; failure attempts retained; no public-package or diversity claim. |\n\nCandidate A totals 19 and B totals 20 under this parent rubric. Parent provisionally prefers B. The common generation-handle architecture is genuine convergence. Both independently rejected cache-owned retirement because it couples storage with subscription policy. The parent reread both losing design alternatives and finds no basis to average them into a resource manager.\n\nCandidate A contributes a useful event-loop trace assertion and unchanged original-probe regression check. If B remains the base after judgment, these checks can be adapted by hand into parent-owned synthesis tests. A's implementation adds no missing ownership invariant to B, so no implementation splice is justified. Keep B's async callback adoption and naming. Reject A's synchronous-only callback restriction, the timer workaround, a missing-value guard, and cache-managed retirement for this fixture.\n\nCandidate files remain unchanged. Every result identity, full input revision and exact structured method matched. Parent read all implementation and helper source, tests, full rationale, source traces, design alternatives, execution receipts and failed attempts. It reran all tests through Bun from actual files. Both have the same observed model value of unknown. This supports independent worker operations, not model diversity.\n"
    },

      "text": "# Parent preliminary candidate A review\n\nAll proposed source, behavioral tests, baseline preparation and execution scripts, finalizer, traced model, design alternatives, rationale, result metadata and receipts were inspected. The combined result/source read truncated a few words in HOW.md, so HOW.md was reread in full separately.\n\nCandidate A uses a fresh private record for every put. The returned frozen handle reads its value and removes the entry only if still current. mount retains this handle, deletes its own current publication synchronously, schedules the pinned disposer once, and returns a stable Promise. A synchronous callback throw rejects that Promise after cleanup. The report callback must be synchronous; its returned Promise is intentionally ignored and documented.\n\nProvisional criterion scores on the parent rubric are 4 for ordering, 4 for ownership, 3 for caller clarity and extension cost, 4 for behavioral evidence, and 4 for claim discipline. The extension cost deduction reflects the manually wired deferred Promise and explicit synchronous-callback restriction. These are artifact selection scores, not a workflow grade.\n\nThe original API's put and invalidate return values change from undefined to handle and boolean. The provided caller remains compatible after its local update; wider compatibility is unknown. The private entry record correctly distinguishes two puts of the identical value object. Reentrant teardown returns the original Promise. Callback failure is observed by explicit await or catch, and ignored failures can be unhandled rejections.\n\nParent execution used actual files without invoking receipt-writing runners in the worker directory. The parent wrote its receipts only to outputs/candidate-a-parent-execution.json. No worker artifacts were modified. There was no retry.\n"

      "text": "import {appendFileSync,readFileSync,writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {resolve} from 'node:path';\nconst p=resolve('outputs');\nconst events=()=>readFileSync(p+'/events.jsonl','utf8').trim().split('\\n').filter(Boolean).map(JSON.parse);\nexport function event(event,details={}) {const order=events().at(-1).order+1;appendFileSync(p+'/events.jsonl',JSON.stringify({order,event,at:new Date().toISOString(),...details})+'\\n');return order;}\nexport function bind(id,workerId,dir,role='candidate',taskArtifact='outputs/candidate-task.md',method=JSON.parse(readFileSync(p+'/method.json'))) {\nconst a={id,scope:role==='candidate'?'Independent cache API design and executable repair':role==='judge'?'Read-only criterion scoring of completed candidates':'Controlled dropout only',inputRevision:'e07d98d277a3c055b09f5b5736626ec9db21cbad',outputOwner:workerId,isolatedOutputPath:resolve('outputs',dir),role,retryCount:0,terminalStatus:'pending',evidenceLinks:[taskArtifact,`outputs/${dir}/RESULT.JSON`]};\nappendFileSync(p+'/assignments.jsonl',JSON.stringify(a)+'\\n');event('assignment-bound',{assignment:a,method});\nreturn a;}\nif(process.argv[2]==='bind-candidates') {bind('candidate-a','/root/candidate_a','candidate-a');bind('candidate-b','/root/candidate_b','candidate-b');bind('dropout','/root/dropout','dropout','dropout');event('candidate-a-dispatch-declared',{assignmentId:'candidate-a'});}\n"

      "text": "# Availability branch\n\nTwo candidate spawns and the controlled dropout spawn succeeded using inherit-parent. No actual unavailable-worker rejection has been observed. Only own-run messages and outputs are used. No private transcript search was performed.       "text": "import { mount } from './caller.mjs';\nlet error;\nprocess.once('uncaughtException', caught => { error = caught; });\nconst unmount = mount('fixture-key', ready => console.log('disposed ready:', ready));\nunmount();\nawait new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 5));\nconsole.log(error ? `teardown error: ${error.name}: ${error.message}` : 'teardown completed');\nprocess.exitCode = error ? 1 : 0;\n"

      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/synthesis/scenarios.mjs",
      "bytes": 4415,
      "sha256": "edc2adf54727dbacd2a816f5114bc9be7a4bb33c4129f0ed39ab30c9ce2136af",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import assert from 'node:assert/strict';\nimport { mount } from './caller.mjs';\nimport { get, put, invalidate } from './cache.mjs';\n\nconst scenario = process.argv[2];\nif (scenario === 'normal') {\n  const reports = [];\n  const unmount = mount('normal', ready => reports.push(ready));\n  assert.equal(get('normal').ready, true);\n  const done = unmount();\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, []);\n  await done;\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [true]);\n  assert.equal(get('normal'), undefined);\n  console.log('PASS normal teardown reports [true] after deferred callback');\n} else if (scenario === 'immediate') {\n  const lease = put('direct', { ready: true });\n  assert.equal(get('direct').ready, true);\n  assert.equal(invalidate('direct'), true);\n  assert.equal(get('direct'), undefined);\n  assert.equal(lease.release(), false);\n  let reported;\n  const unmount = mount('immediate', ready => { reported = ready; });\n  assert.equal(get('immediate').ready, true);\n  const done = unmount();\n  assert.equal(get('immediate'), undefined);\n  assert.equal(reported, undefined);\n  await done;\n  assert.equal(reported, true);\n  const invalidatedUnmount = mount('admin', ready => { reported = ready; });\n  assert.equal(invalidate('admin'), true);\n  await invalidatedUnmount();\n  assert.equal(reported, true);\n  console.log('PASS immediate invalidate and unmount remove current entry before return');\n} else if (scenario === 'replacement') {\n  const reports = [];\n  const oldUnmount = mount('replacement', value => reports.push(['old', value]));\n  const newUnmount = mount('replacement', value => reports.push(['new', value]));\n  const current = get('replacement');\n  const oldDone = oldUnmount();\n  assert.equal(get('replacement'), current);\n  await oldDone;\n  assert.equal(get('replacement'), current);\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [['old', true]]);\n  await newUnmount();\n  assert.equal(get('replacement'), undefined);\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [['old', true], ['new', true]]);\n  const value = { ready: true };\n  const first = put('same-object', value);\n  const second = put('same-object', value);\n  assert.equal(first.release(), false);\n  assert.equal(get('same-object'), value);\n  assert.equal(second.release(), true);\n  const retainedReports = [];\n  const retainedUnmount = mount('different-value', ready => retainedReports.push(ready));\n  const replacement = put('different-value', { ready: false });\n  await retainedUnmount();\n  assert.deepEqual(retainedReports, [true]);\n  assert.equal(get('different-value').ready, false);\n  assert.equal(replacement.release(), true);\n  const queuedUnmount = mount('queued-replacement', ready => retainedReports.push(ready));\n  const done = queuedUnmount();\n  const queuedLease = put('queued-replacement', { ready: false });\n  await done;\n  assert.equal(get('queued-replacement').ready, false);\n  assert.deepEqual(retainedReports, [true, true]);\n  assert.equal(queuedLease.release(), true);\n  console.log('PASS same-key replacements survive old teardown, including same-value identity and queued replacement');\n} else if (scenario === 'repeat-and-errors') {\n  const reports = [];\n  const unmount = mount('repeat', ready => reports.push(ready));\n  const done = unmount();\n  assert.equal(unmount(), done);\n  await done;\n  assert.equal(unmount(), done);\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [true]);\n  for (const kind of ['throw', 'reject']) {\n    const failure = new Error(`report ${kind}`);\n    let calls = 0;\n    let failingUnmount;\n    failingUnmount = mount(kind, ready => {\n      calls += 1;\n      assert.equal(ready, true);\n      assert.equal(failingUnmount(), failingDone);\n      if (kind === 'throw') throw failure;\n      return Promise.reject(failure);\n    });\n    assert.equal(get(kind).ready, true);\n    const failingDone = failingUnmount();\n    assert.equal(get(kind), undefined);\n    assert.equal(failingUnmount(), failingDone);\n    await assert.rejects(failingDone, error => error === failure);\n    assert.equal(failingUnmount(), failingDone);\n    await assert.rejects(failingUnmount(), error => error === failure);\n    assert.equal(calls, 1);\n    const recovery = mount(kind, ready => reports.push(ready));\n    await recovery();\n  }\n  assert.deepEqual(reports, [true, true, true]);\n  console.log('PASS repeated teardown reports once and returns same promise; sync throws and rejected reports reject it without retaining cache entries');\n} else {\n  throw new Error(`Unknown scenario ${scenario}`);\n}\n"

      "text": "# Parent synthesis decision and evidence\n\nCandidate B is the base. The fresh judge and parent independently preferred its generation-specific `release()` name and completion promise that adopts report callbacks' returned promises. Both found A correct under its explicitly narrower synchronous-callback contract. Parent scores and rationales are in ../parent-comparison.md. The exact captured judge response is ../judge/RESULT.JSON. These are candidate-selection judgments, not workflow grades.\n\nBoth candidates independently converged on a fresh private record per insertion, one retained value per mount, and identity-conditional removal. Reusing a value object cannot confuse insertion authority. The parent reread A's complete rationale, ownership design and tests after comparing B. No implementation graft is needed for the common ownership design. The selected cache.mjs and caller.mjs retain B's implementation unchanged.\n\nThe parent hand-adapted A's explicit callback-order assertion into additional-checks.mjs and retained the unchanged original input/probe.mjs as probe.mjs. The parent also added the judge's missing delayed-fulfillment check using a controlled promise gate. A further assertion verifies that a replacement created by a failing report survives. These additions share B's existing API and do not add another lifecycle mechanism. scenarios.mjs retains B's four scenario groups as the base verification artifact.\n\nThe optional lazy-completion implementation graft suggested by the judge was rejected. B's explicit started flag and stable completion promise are already verified. Removing one internal state variable would introduce a new implementation combination without a required behavioral benefit. This is a limited disagreement over optional simplification, not over base selection. The parent also rejects A's synchronous-only report restriction, timer restoration, missing-value guards, value-identity deletion, and cache-owned retirement. Their concrete reasons are recorded in the parent comparison and judge result.\n\nThe synthesis satisfies the same artifact rubric within the fixture. The failing after-state and successful timer baseline establish the ordering diagnosis. Four inherited scenario groups and additional checks establish immediate removal, retained true reporting, old-handle replacement safety, same-object reuse, repeat/reentrant completion, callback failures and delayed fulfillment. The README makes current-key invalidation distinct from generation release, and states the API return changes. The exact vendor bytes match the manifest. No public-package, memory-reclamation, multi-thread or production-compatibility claim follows.\n\nThe judge identified a completion dependency cycle when a report returns its own teardown promise. The parent reproduced that completion remaining pending at two microtask checkpoints. Source inspection explains the dependency; the bounded observation does not prove arbitrary elapsed-time behavior. README.md explicitly forbids callbacks from depending on their own teardown. Never-settling reports also leave completion pending. No cycle detection or timeout was added. Callers must handle rejected teardown promises, and retained values remain mutable references.\n\n../synthesis-verification.json preserves eight actual Bun commands, stdout, stderr, expected exits and observed exits. The after-state intentionally exits 1. The restored timer and all six synthesis commands exit 0. The unchanged original trigger prints `disposed ready: true` and `teardown completed`. ../verify-synthesis.mjs reruns the actual files without installation.\n\nA separate actual third worker, /root/dropout, returned blocked under an explicitly controlled assignment that withheld implementation. Its RESULT.JSON and own checklist remain in ../dropout, and no partial candidate work entered synthesis. That branch demonstrates a controlled dropout, not host failure. No actual unavailable-worker rejection was observed. The unavailable-worker variant remains unverified/blocked in ../availability.md. Exactly two surviving candidates competed.\n\nThe native host returned canonical worker identities but no actual model or effort values. Requests inherited the parent. Models, model diversity and verified different-model trail review remain unobserved. The source reports package version 0.1.3, but its cache directory is not a Git repository, so the supplied freeze SHA cannot be independently derived there. The task input SHA was independently verified. All candidate output hashes remain unchanged after parent and judge inspection. No retry or retrospective result-metadata repair occurred.\n\nThe initial dropout method event mistakenly copied the surviving-candidate method. The actual dropout dispatch supplied its separate one-sample method and the worker echoed it. The original event remains, with a correction appended at order 13. The dropout branch stays outside the competitive method receipt. Candidate B's recorder setup and supplemental hash-input failures remain preserved. Neither was hidden or counted as a completed behavioral check.\n\nSeparate Before Serializing Shared State assigned distinct directories before dispatch. Model the Domain selected explicit insertion ownership. Fix Root Causes rejected lookup guards. Laziness Protocol kept the two-module boundary. Redesign From First Principles kept grafts coherent. Test Behavior, Not Implementation required literal state and payload assertions. Prove It Works required actual parent reruns. Build the Lever produced verify-synthesis.mjs. Exhaust the Design Space required whole ownership alternatives before selection. Their frozen leaves were read this session.\n\n## Attention\n\nreviewed by unknown\n\nThe judge is an independent worker, but no different model family was observed.       "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/synthesis/additional-checks.mjs",
      "bytes": 2387,
      "sha256": "7f37db8c94f4684b7a58a55e04432e5325a5ec2b4eaaab812d61fad5a6903af9",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import assert from 'node:assert/strict';\nimport { mount } from './caller.mjs';\nimport { get, put } from './cache.mjs';\n\nconst trace = [];\nconst stop = mount('ordering', ready => trace.push(`reported ${ready}`));\nconst done = stop();\ntrace.push('returned');\nassert.deepEqual(trace, ['returned']);\nqueueMicrotask(() => trace.push('later microtask'));\nawait done;\nassert.deepEqual(trace, ['returned', 'reported true', 'later microtask']);\nconsole.log('Deferred report order matches the original scheduling contract.');\n\nlet finishReport;\nconst gate = new Promise(resolve => { finishReport = resolve; });\nconst asyncReports = [];\nconst asyncStop = mount('async-success', async ready => {\n  asyncReports.push(['started', ready]);\n  await gate;\n  asyncReports.push(['finished', ready]);\n});\nconst asyncDone = asyncStop();\nassert.equal(get('async-success'), undefined);\nassert.equal(asyncStop(), asyncDone);\nlet settled = false;\nasyncDone.then(() => { settled = true; });\nawait Promise.resolve();\nassert.deepEqual(asyncReports, [['started', true]]);\nassert.equal(settled, false);\nfinishReport();\nawait asyncDone;\nassert.deepEqual(asyncReports, [['started', true], ['finished', true]]);\nassert.equal(settled, true);\nconsole.log('Completion waits for a controlled asynchronous report to fulfill.');\n\nconst failure = new Error('replacement from report');\nlet replacement;\nconst failingStop = mount('callback-replacement', ready => {\n  assert.equal(ready, true);\n  replacement = put('callback-replacement', { ready: 'new owner' });\n  throw failure;\n});\nawait assert.rejects(failingStop(), error => error === failure);\nassert.deepEqual(get('callback-replacement'), { ready: 'new owner' });\nassert.equal(replacement.release(), true);\nconsole.log('A report-created replacement survives the old callback failure.');\n\nlet cycleStop;\nlet cycleReported = false;\ncycleStop = mount('cycle-limit', ready => {\n  assert.equal(ready, true);\n  cycleReported = true;\n  return cycleStop();\n});\nconst cycleDone = cycleStop();\nlet cycleSettled = false;\ncycleDone.then(() => { cycleSettled = true; }, () => { cycleSettled = true; });\nawait Promise.resolve();\nawait Promise.resolve();\nassert.equal(cycleReported, true);\nassert.equal(get('cycle-limit'), undefined);\nassert.equal(cycleSettled, false);\nconsole.log('Known limit observed at two microtask checkpoints: returning own teardown completion remains pending.');\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/synthesis/README.md",
      "bytes": 2355,
      "sha256": "0f590837869b76bc44c06e49f49059a4a98f649381af2728009a5b2942b35796",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Cache API with insertion-owned teardown\n\nUse one mount handle per subscription. Calling it removes its current insertion synchronously. Await its returned promise to observe report completion or failure.\n\n```js\nimport { mount } from './caller.mjs';\nconst unmount = mount('panel', ready => console.log(ready));\nawait unmount();\n```\n\nThe cache stores a private generation record for every `put`, even when a caller reuses the same value object. `put(key, value)` returns a frozen `{ value, release() }` handle. `release()` deletes only that insertion if it is still current. `invalidate(key)` deletes the current key unconditionally and synchronously. Both deletion operations return booleans. `get(key)` returns the current value or undefined.\n\nThe mount retains its own handle. The exact pinned disposer queues its callback as a microtask. That callback reads its retained value rather than looking up the current key. Old teardown cannot remove a replacement. Repeated and reentrant teardown return one completion promise and queue one callback. Synchronous throws and returned promise rejections reject that completion after cache removal. Fulfilled asynchronous reports settle completion only after they finish.\n\nThis changes the original fixture's ignored return values. `put` now returns a handle, invalidation returns a boolean, and teardown returns a promise. No caller compatibility beyond the supplied fixture is established.\n\nReport callbacks must settle independently of their own teardown. A callback that returns or awaits its own teardown completion creates a dependency cycle. A callback that never settles also leaves completion pending. No timeout or cycle policy is implemented. Consumers must observe rejected completion promises. Callback side effects are neither retried nor rolled back. Retained values remain mutable references.\n\nRun the full local comparison from the task root with `bun --no-install outputs/verify-synthesis.mjs`. It executes the actual after-state and restored timer baselines, four ownership scenarios, additional timing and asynchronous checks, and the unchanged supplied probe. The vendor is an exact local fixture, not a public dependency compatibility claim.\n\nThe adjacent `NOTE.md` records parent selection, judge findings, test grafts, rejected alternatives, dropout and unverified capability branches.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/synthesis/caller.mjs",
      "bytes": 780,
      "sha256": "026b444e819ce47824006882a0d77b3b5c60f467d587a355920672e4ad4aff74",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { createDisposer } from './vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\nimport { put } from './cache.mjs';\n\nexport function mount(key, report) {\n  const lease = put(key, { ready: true });\n  let resolveCompletion;\n  let rejectCompletion;\n  const completion = new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveCompletion = resolve;\n    rejectCompletion = reject;\n  });\n  const subscription = createDisposer(() => {\n    try {\n      Promise.resolve(report(lease.value.ready)).then(\n        () => resolveCompletion(),\n        rejectCompletion,\n      );\n    } catch (error) {\n      rejectCompletion(error);\n    }\n  });\n  let started = false;\n  return function unmount() {\n    if (!started) {\n      started = true;\n      subscription.dispose();\n      lease.release();\n    }\n    return completion;\n  };\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/synthesis/cache.mjs",
      "bytes": 415,
      "sha256": "caecdb40208c618d6e92bdd85e113fee84f7c27769876abf041d8aa8308200da",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "const entries = new Map();\n\nexport function put(key, value) {\n  const generation = { value };\n  entries.set(key, generation);\n  return Object.freeze({\n    value,\n    release() {\n      if (entries.get(key) !== generation) return false;\n      return entries.delete(key);\n    },\n  });\n}\n\nexport function get(key) {\n  return entries.get(key)?.value;\n}\n\nexport function invalidate(key) {\n  return entries.delete(key);\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/synthesis/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs",
      "bytes": 196,
      "sha256": "d33e8d36600ad5df008657e478e39f9100a347098b4176f37923e5056cb3427f",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "// Exact vendored fixture dependency implementation, pinned by local SHA-256 in manifest.json.\nexport function createDisposer(onDispose) {\n  return { dispose: () => queueMicrotask(onDispose) };\n}\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/before-state/probe.mjs",
      "bytes": 395,
      "sha256": "fa8cdce0fe97a6e3ed1b071aceab4365938b862c335cfccdb94508173a7fd002",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { mount } from './caller.mjs';\nlet error;\nprocess.once('uncaughtException', caught => { error = caught; });\nconst unmount = mount('fixture-key', ready => console.log('disposed ready:', ready));\nunmount();\nawait new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 5));\nconsole.log(error ? `teardown error: ${error.name}: ${error.message}` : 'teardown completed');\nprocess.exitCode = error ? 1 : 0;\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/before-state/caller.mjs",
      "bytes": 309,
      "sha256": "9566a520d6763d359c4b43cd5705df6333c8bf4a4afb96aec7ac3ca2e2145490",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { createDisposer } from './vendor/disposer-v1.mjs';\nimport { get, put, invalidate } from './cache.mjs';\nexport function mount(key, report) {\n  put(key, { ready: true });\n  const subscription = createDisposer(() => report(get(key).ready));\n  return () => { subscription.dispose(); invalidate(key); };\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/before-state/cache.mjs",
      "bytes": 220,
      "sha256": "b9ff3cf490c78675ef169324135630dcd5741dbd031de81d2798f7c67c020dea",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "const entries = new Map();\nexport function put(key, value) { entries.set(key, value); }\nexport function get(key) { return entries.get(key); }\nexport function invalidate(key) { setTimeout(() => entries.delete(key), 0); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "strict-method-repairs/candidate-cross-review/outputs/before-state/vendor/disposer-v1.mjs",
      "bytes": 196,
      "sha256": "d33e8d36600ad5df008657e478e39f9100a347098b4176f37923e5056cb3427f",
      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "// Exact vendored fixture dependency implementation, pinned by local SHA-256 in manifest.json.\nexport function createDisposer(onDispose) {\n  return { dispose: () => queueMicrotask(onDispose) };\n}\n"
    },

      "owner": "local-013-strict-candidate-cross-review",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Execution checklist\n\n- [x] Read full contract, worker role, strict validator, and input.\n- [x] Verify task Git SHA and frozen package identity.\n- [x] Read frozen mode, principles, README, linked arena workflow, skill, and orchestration references.\n- [x] Inspect exposed tool documentation and record capability/permission limitations.\n- [x] Frame cache API problem and create parent-only rubric and shared task.\n- [x] Bootstrap and bind two isolated candidate workers before dispatch; run both concurrently.\n- [x] Exercise separate actual dropout candidate and unavailable-worker branch honestly.\n- [x] Inspect all terminal candidate artifacts and execute Bun tests/probes.\n- [x] Bootstrap, bind, and dispatch fresh read-only judge after inspection.\n- [x] Inspect judge scoring, resolve disagreements, synthesize and verify.\n- [x] Write receipt, operation ledger, event log, manifest, completion record and adjacent note.\n\n## Arena phases, verbatim\n\n- [x] 1. Frame\n- [x] 2. Fan out\n- [x] 3. Cross-judge\n- [x] 4. Pick\n- [x] 5. Graft\n- [x] 6. Verify\n\n## Throughput checkpoint\n\n- Blocking first steps. Full reads, exact SHA, exposed APIs, rubric and isolation precede dispatch.\n- Independent workstreams. Two candidate directories and separate controlled dropout.\n- Shared mutable state. Parent alone writes assignments, events and synthesis.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. Two complete ownership designs, one later read-only judge.\n\n## Terminal limitations\n\nAll local phases produced the referenced artifacts. The unavailable-worker variant remains unverified/blocked rather than an observed rejection. The controlled dropout is separate. Package freeze Git derivation, actual models, complete transcript audit and cross-model trail review remain unverified. Missing companion gates remain disclosed. No workflow grade is assigned.\n"
    },
